[[sep-dv-verification-plan]]
== SEP OCAH Open-Source Verification Plan

=== Overview

[cols=",",options="header"]
|===
|Field |Value
|Design Under Test |`sep_wrapper` (`hw/top/sep_wrapper.sv` + `sep_ip_integration`)
|Bare core |`sep` (`hw/sys/sep/rtl/sep.sv`)
|Repository |`tt-oca-harness`
|Project |OCAH
|Framework |PyUVM on cocotb (Phase 1, Phase 2 and Phase 3); SystemVerilog UVM smoke set on the same top (`--framework uvm`, VCS)
|Primary simulator |Verilator
|Design specification |`hw/sys/sep/doc/index.adoc` and the chapters it includes
|Register sources |`hw/sys/sep/regs/` (SystemRDL); the generated export `hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/`, read through `cocotb/env/sep_reg_meta.py`
|How to run |`README.md` in this DV tree (`tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut sep`)
|Cocotb tests |Every member of the `all` group in `testlists/all.toml`; its `expected_count` is the authoritative number
|SV-UVM smoke set |Every test with a `uvm` binding (`--framework uvm --items all`); see <<sv-uvm-smoke>>
|===

This VPLAN is the technical contract for the open-source SEP DV
suite: what each phase covers, how tests are named, which VIPs
drive the DUT, and what each registered test must prove. It is
written for adopters and maintainers of the public tree. Testbench
structure (env hierarchy, HDL top, bring-up helpers) is in
<<sep-dv-testbench-architecture,SEP_TB_ARCH>>.

PASS/FAIL needs positive evidence: a passing `results.xml` and named
`CHK-<ID> PASS` lines in the leaf log (<<sep-vplan-evidence-gate>>). A clean
simulator exit alone is not enough.

=== Verification Strategy

One DUT. Three phases on cocotb, plus a SystemVerilog UVM smoke set
(see <<sv-uvm-smoke>>).

[cols="1,2,3",options="header"]
|===
|Phase |Environment |Job

|Phase 1
|cocotb / PyUVM + Verilator
|Smoke plus subsystem representatives. Fewest tests that carry the most real datapath behaviour.

|Phase 2
|Same cocotb environment
|Iconic feature coverage by IP, plus cross-IP edges a per-IP bench cannot see. Includes block activation for instantiated IPs that have no other vehicle. Randomize when there is a legal matrix (`RAND-REP` / `RANDCFG`); keep directed (`RAND-NONE`) when there is not.

|Phase 3
|Same cocotb environment. A test that reads state that only a four-state simulator shows pins VCS.
|Residual contracts: the contracts of the specification and the register description that no Phase 1 or Phase 2 check grades. Corner and illegal cells, injected faults, reset and lock behaviour, and drawn matrices. A Phase 3 test never overlaps a Phase 1 or Phase 2 test. A test that is a superset of an open test names the cell that it adds. Randomize when there is a legal matrix (`RAND-REP` / `RANDCFG`); keep directed (`RAND-NONE`) when there is not. Every expected value comes from the specification and the register description, never from the RTL and never from another test. A check that rests on a PROPOSED internal drive is blocked and takes no credit. A read-only observation tap, an error inject and a drive of a `sep` port are not PROPOSED. A test with no executable check is parked (<<sep-vplan-credit-rules>>, <<sep-vplan-phase3-parked>>).
|===

Phase 1, Phase 2 and Phase 3 are scope tiers, not a schedule. Phase 1 is the
bring-up and representative set. Phase 2 is the dense, breadth and fault set.
Phase 3 is the residual set. Each test entry names its phase. `SEP_FCOV.adoc`
has its own Phase 1 / Phase 2 taxonomy: Phase 1 holds the basic-mode groups and
Phase 2 the dense groups; an FCOV phase does not follow the phase of its owning
test. The FCOV Phase 2 groups are the coverage of the Phase 3 tests. A tier
says what a test is asked to prove. It does not say when the test is written.
A Phase 3 entry takes credit under the rules of <<sep-vplan-credit-rules>>.
The contracts this plan does not claim are in <<sep-vplan-scope>>. The Phase 3
contracts that no test can grade are in <<sep-vplan-phase3-lists>>. The
observation taps, the added hooks and the PROPOSED drives of the Phase 3 tests
are in <<sep-vplan-phase3-proposed>>. The parked-test rule of Phase 3 is in
<<sep-vplan-phase3-parked>>.

[arabic]
. *Stimulus*: no-CPU tests drive the CPU-LSU AXI splice through
  `ocah_axi_vip` / `SepAxiAgent`. An optional second master drives the
  real SMN-inbound port (traverses the inbound filter). CPU tests boot
  VeeR EL2 firmware from ICCM. SPI flash uses `ocah_spi_vip`.
  A test whose target is `lsu_stub_all_live` runs on two builds.
  The Verilator build is `lsu_stub_all_live`: `shims/cpu/sep_cpu_stub.sv`
  replaces `sep_cpu` and drives the LSU request. The VCS build, which
  produces the graded logs, is `default`, where `run_on` in
  `[targets.lsu_stub_all_live.tools.vcs]` sends the test: the full VeeR
  core is held off and `tb/tb_top.sv` force-splices the VIP onto the same
  LSU request node (the no_cpu force-splice block). Both builds present
  the request at that node, so a test whose checked path is downstream of
  it covers both builds. A test whose checked path includes `sep_cpu`
  itself names the build its evidence comes from.
. *Checking*: frontdoor reads/writes, independent Python goldens where
  needed (AES/HMAC/KMAC/OTBN/entropy/LCC), and named checkers that can
  fail on a broken DUT. Prefer source-derived expected values from the
  register export and architecture docs.
. *Bring-up*: drive `ext_boot_seq_done_i`. A test that does not need
  fuse content may use `+skip_fuse_sense`; it must then grade no fuse- or
  LC-derived value. <<sep-standin-skip-fuse-sense>> records the claim and
  scope of this bypass for every leaf that uses it. Hold the CPU off for
  `no_cpu` runs. With `+skip_fuse_sense` and no
  `+sep_shadow_reg_preload`, the eFuse shadow is all zero except
  `LC_STATE` raw `4'hF`, which is INVALID. `FEAT_CTRL` is then zero, so
  `sep_debug` is 0 and the inbound filter is in the SMN-inbound path. A
  skip-mode test that sends SMN-inbound traffic programs a filter window,
  and a deny check that `FEAT_CTRL` = 0 would also produce does not prove
  a filter rule.
. *Coverage*: the feature matrix (<<iconic-scorecard>>) for the
  cocotb phases. VCS Phase 1 functional-coverage bins live in
  <<sep-dv-functional-coverage,SEP_FCOV>> (`u_sep_fcov`). SystemVerilog covergroups
  are not compiled on the Verilator acceptance path. RTL code coverage
  is a separate, unthresholded number (<<sep-fcov-evidence-paths>>).

Randomization contract: the *same* config object programs the DUT and the
golden. Pin the datapath directed first, then upgrade that test to randomized —
do not ship a one-point stand-in for a matrix. Skip randomization only when the
feature has no legal knob space (a strap, a single named handshake).

[[audit]]
Checker contracts in this plan are written against these known ways a check can
pass while the DUT is unable to fail it.

*An expectation that shares a source with the thing it measures.* A golden must
not be selected by asking the engine which candidate it agrees with. The HMAC
software-key word order is pinned from the IP register specification:
`vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/hmac/data/hmac.hjson` (the `KEY` multireg) states
`key[1023:0] = {KEY0, KEY1, ... , KEY31}`, so `KEY_0` is the most-significant
word and the software path needs no reversal. The RTL agrees
(`hmac.sv` assigns the key CSRs into the secret-key vector in reverse
index order), and that agreement is corroboration rather than the source: a
golden transcribed from the RTL cannot disagree with the RTL. A two-candidate
scan is legitimate only as a failure diagnostic that separates a word-order
regression from a general digest fault.

*Golden compared against golden.* An assert between a result already pinned
bit-exact to its golden and another output of the same model holds with the
simulator switched off. The degenerate case compares list lengths rather than
domains — a four-word wrong-mode golden against an eight-word digest.

*A guard whose own constructor makes it unfalsifiable.* Non-vacuity must not
rest on a relation the configuration builder already guarantees: an unwritten
(zero-initialised) address required to differ from a pattern built as
`getrandbits(64) | 1`, or `start_lo != 0` where the config builds that value as
`(random & ~0xFFF) or 0x1000`.

*A stimulus with no fan-out.* Driving a pin that is unread in the configuration
under test and asserting that two observables did not move is a DV-invented
scenario, not a specification claim. `dbg_rstb_i` is routed only into
`sep_cpu`, so it has no observable in a CPU-stub run, and
`hw/sys/sep/doc/port_table.adoc` does not require isolation from system reset.

*A tolerance wider than the signal.* A post-event bound above the count the
window actually reaches passes when the event is ignored. The window must run
long enough that the pre-event count clears the tolerance by a wide margin.

*A check that reads back nothing.* Walking a register through several encodings
without reading it back cannot distinguish a register stuck at its reset value
when those encodings produce byte-identical output for the chosen stimulus.

*Entailed guards.* The absence of a complementary-arm marker, or a deferred
line, is not a checker; the owning test already fails if a forbidden marker
appears.

[[sep-vplan-evidence-gate]]
==== Evidence gate

Per-run logs land in `build/runs/<run-id>/` (git-ignored). PASS/FAIL requires
positive evidence from `results.xml`. A clean simulator exit alone is not
enough. A test passing is the entry condition for reading its checkers, never a
substitute for them. A checker row exists only if a run can prove it. A log tag
is not the proof.

A test that exits cleanly without checking anything is not a pass, and
`sep_base_test` makes such a run fail. Every check logs `CHK-<ID> PASS`. The
base class counts the distinct IDs that a leaf emits and prints one line per
test:

----
EVIDENCE_SUMMARY test=<name> observed=N own=N required=N missing=N ids=...
----

`own` is the observed set less the IDs in `_EvidenceFilter.BASE_IDS`. That set
is empty, so every record counts toward `own`, the shared records listed under
Test Procedures included. A leaf whose `own` count is zero fails. Firmware
leaves emit `CHK-FW-CONSOLE` from `poll_boot` after the mailbox PASS magic, or
`CHK-VERDICT` after `cold_scratch[0]` reports completion on the scratch0-gated
leaves. `_EvidenceFilter.NO_OWN_EVIDENCE` names the leaves that may pass with no
own record. It is empty, and a new entry hides a logging gap.

A leaf can declare more: `min_evidence = N` sets a floor on `own`, and
`required_evidence = ("CHK-A", ...)` names IDs that must appear.

The gate proves that a check ran. It does not prove that the check was right.

A row names its log lines by the prefixes of <<sep-log-prefixes>>: a test
_grades_ a `CHK-` line, _controls_ with a `CTRL-` line, _logs as an open
question_ an `OBS-` line, and _records_ an `INFO-` line. Only `CHK-` and
`CTRL-` lines are evidence for a row.

[[bench-assertion-waiver]]
==== Bench-scope assertion waiver

The `[pass_fail]` patterns of `sep_sim_cfg.toml` decide, for every leaf, which
assertion failure the runner reads as a failure. They answer for the whole
bench rather than for one test, so they take this record rather than a run-mode
note on a test entry. A per-test assertion waiver stays a plusarg on its test entry.

[cols="1,4",options="header"]
|===
|Field |Value
|Claim |A leaf graded PASS has had every assertion failure of the run read, apart from the time-0 firing of the two external-ack assertions of the vendored Adams Bridge register block and any other `abr_reg.sv` message at time 0
|Why |`vendor/chipsalliance/adams-bridge/upstream/src/abr_top/rtl/abr_reg.sv` holds two immediate assertions on `external_wr_ack` and `external_rd_ack`, in the post-reset branch of an `always_ff`. On a four-state simulator the first clock edge sees the reset and the ack strobes at X, and each assertion fires once at time 0. The file is vendored third-party RTL, so the tree does not edit it, and `$assertoff` in the testbench races the time-0 evaluation
|Scope |Two `extra_ignore_patterns` entries and one `extra_hard_fail_patterns` entry. The ignore entries drop an `abr_reg.sv` line that reports time 0 and a line that names `abr_reg.sv` line 67 or 69. The hard-fail entry matches the timestamped line of the same two assertions with a non-zero `failed at` time. The runner matches hard-fail patterns over the raw log, whatever the ignore patterns drop, so a later firing fails the leaf. No other assertion is touched
|Closes when |The vendored block stops asserting on an X ack strobe, or the reset and the ack strobes into it are at known values at the first clock edge. Either removes the three patterns
|===

Re-confirm: `grep -nF 'abr_reg\.sv' hw/sys/sep/dv/sep_sim_cfg.toml` prints
exactly three pattern lines: two under `extra_ignore_patterns` and one under
`extra_hard_fail_patterns` that carries `failed at [1-9]`. Any other output
means that the record is stale, and the record is what needs the edit.

=== Naming Rules

[cols=",",options="header"]
|===
|Rule |Requirement
|Test case name |`sep_<feature>_<scenario>_test` in lower snake case
|Single-scenario module |Module name matches the test case
|Plusarg-selected variant |Several testlist entries may share one module when each selects its scenario with a plusarg. The entry name then does NOT end the module name, so the entry must carry the plusarg in `args` and a comment naming the scenario. The variants in the tree are `sep_efuse_secure_tm_write_lock_test` (`efuse.sep_locked_field_access_irq_path_test`, `+secure_tm_lock`), `sep_esrc_e2e_fifo_closed_test` (`system.sep_esrc_e2e_smoke_test`, `+esrc_fifo_closed`) and the seven `sep_lcc_lc_state_w1s_*_test` (`lcc.sep_lcc_lc_state_transition_matrix_test`, `+lc_start=N`). Use it to avoid copying one implementation per scenario, not to hide a missing module. A Phase 3 test has one testlist entry, and a second one only when the run mode differs (<<sep-vplan-phase3-drawn>>)
|Multi-scenario / RAND-REP |One leaf test walks every required discrete cell in one invocation; do not silently ship a one-point stand-in for a matrix. A Phase 3 leaf walks only cheap, contract-critical cells and draws the other cells from its seed (<<sep-vplan-phase3-drawn>>)
|Open-source files |PyUVM test under `cocotb/tests/`, stimulus in `cocotb/seq_lib/`, shared helpers on `sep_base_test` / `cocotb/env/`
|Testlist enrollment |Leaf entry in the owning `testlists/*.toml` and in `testlists/all.toml`
|Evidence |Every checker needs a positive log line naming the contract, as `CHK-<ID> PASS`
|Evidence gate |A clean exit is not a pass: every graded contract logs `CHK-<ID> PASS`, and `sep_base_test` fails a leaf that emits no `CHK-<ID> PASS` record. The shared base-class records count toward that floor, so a leaf whose own checks sit beside them names them in `required_evidence`. <<sep-vplan-evidence-gate>> describes the mechanism, its `EVIDENCE_SUMMARY` line, the `min_evidence` / `required_evidence` knobs and the exemption list
|===

=== BFM Policy

[cols=",",options="header"]
|===
|Interface / Protocol |Open-Source Replacement
|CPU-LSU AXI (no_cpu) |`ocah_axi_vip` via `SepAxiAgent` on the LSU splice
|SMN-inbound AXI |Second `SepAxiAgent` on the real inbound port
|SMN-outbound AXI |`tb/sep_outbound_mbx.sv`: firmware console and PASS/FAIL mailbox monitor. It answers every outbound access OKAY, and every read with zero data, for any address. A test whose check depends on an outbound response names it under *BFM / Model*.
|SPI flash |`ocah_spi_vip` `OcahSpiFlash`
|JTAG (eFuse mux path) |`ocah_axi_vip` AXI-Lite master on the OTP JTAG2AXIL port (`j_axi_*`)
|Entropy noise / goldens |TB noise port + pure-Python goldens in `cocotb/env/`
|eFuse / OTP |Generic OSS eFuse model (no foundry OTP)
|Memories |Wrapper macros + sim-time image load
|===

[[sep-vplan-scope]]
=== Verification Scope

This plan claims SEP behaviour at the `sep_wrapper` boundary of the
open-source build: the Bender targets `sep`, `sep_el2` and `sep_wrapper`, with
the DV shims and models that <<sep-dv-testbench-architecture,SEP_TB_ARCH>>
describes. The behaviours below are outside this plan. A green SEP regression
says nothing about them.

[cols="1,2",options="header"]
|===
|Behaviour |Why it is out of scope here
|Boot ROM firmware contracts: the `rom_fw` and `rom_fw_smoke` groups, `testlists/rom_fw.toml` |They have their own owner, the `rom_boot` RTL target and the `boot_rom*` firmware profiles, and they are firmware contracts rather than hardware contracts. They are not members of `all`, so this plan grades none of them. <<sep-rom-dv-verification-plan,SEP_ROM_VPLAN>> grades them
|Proprietary IP |Excluded for OSS hygiene: proprietary IPs outside the public tree. The build uses the Bender targets `sep`, `sep_el2` and `sep_wrapper` only
|Inbound global-to-local remap |`u_inbound_global_to_local_addr_remap` (`hw/sys/sep/rtl/sep_system_peripherals/rtl/sep_system_peripherals.sv`) rewrites inbound addresses in [`SEP_GLOBAL_BASE_ADDR`, +`SEP_REGION_SIZE`) to local addresses after `u_inbound_filter`. No test in `all` grades the translation. `sep_fabric_remap_filter_csr_bank_test` grades the CSR read/write path. The sixteen programmable `axi_alias_remap` regions are a different mechanism, which `sep_fabric_local_alias_datapath_test` grades
|Reset generation and return after a watchdog bite |The integrating reset controller outside this DUT owns them. `sep_reset_wdt_sanity_test` grades the bite request at the SEP output pin
|Entropy quality, ring-oscillator tuning and health tests over real noise |The bench injects noise at the decorrelator input (<<esrc-noise-exception>>). These belong to the entropy IP's own testbench
|Features with no vehicle in this suite: AGP, local UART/GPIO, ROM patch, DMA-AES, DCLS-in-SEP, PCR vault |No test in `all` drives them, and the feature matrix (<<iconic-scorecard>>) has no row for them
|Entropy-pool drain-under-fill |No test in `all` drives it. `sep_entropy_pool_aperture_test` grades the stall duration (`CHK-STALL-DURATION`)
|Drain-before-reset on the AES and OTBN isolation domains |No test in `all` drives it. The HMAC, KMAC, Adams Bridge and shared TRNG domains are graded (<<iconic-scorecard>>)
|Locked-field access interrupt taken by the CPU PIC and its service routine |No test in `all` drives it. `sep_locked_field_access_irq_path_test` grades the interrupt line
|ESRC health-test quality, and the `prim_count` counter-redundancy fault |No test in `all` drives them. The `prim_count` error is reachable only by a physical fault, so it needs fault injection (<<sep_esrc_alert_delivery_test>>)
|Inbound-filter negative product |No test in `all` drives it
|Negative matrices for the CPU LSU, the fabric gaps, the extended IRQ connectivity and the eFuse permissions |No test in `all` drives them
|KMAC sideload fail-closed `START` with no valid key (`ErrKeyNotValid`), and recovery through `CMD.err_processed` |No test in `all` drives it (<<sep_km_kmac_sideload_kat_test>>)
|===

[[sep-vplan-traceability]]
=== Scope and Traceability

This section is the requirement-to-test-to-coverage matrix for SEP. The
requirements are the designer-owned design documentation under
`hw/sys/sep/doc/`. The Test Procedures below state what each test verifies,
`SEP_FCOV.adoc` states the coverage intent, and the live group membership is
`testlists/all.toml` and the per-area lists it includes. This section
reconciles them: which tests count as feature credit, and which features have
no credit on this bench.

[[sep-vplan-source-of-truth]]
==== Source of truth

The DV book carries no specification or register reference of its own. Where
the documents overlap, the table names the one that wins.

[cols="2,3,3",options="header"]
|===
|Question |Source of truth |Disposition of the other documents
|What SEP must do |The design documentation under `hw/sys/sep/doc/` |This plan cites it. A plan statement that contradicts a design chapter is a plan defect
|Register layouts |The SystemRDL under `hw/sys/sep/regs/` and the generated export under `hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/` |`cocotb/env/sep_reg_meta.py` reads the generated export, and the register metadata that the tests use comes from it
|Which tests exist |`testlists/all.toml` and the per-area lists it includes (`system.toml`, `cpu.toml`, `memory.toml`, `km.toml`, `crypto.toml`, `efuse_lcc.toml`, `spi.toml`, `rom_fw.toml`) |The Test Procedures document every member of `all`. A name in one and not the other is a defect in whichever lacks it, except a Phase 3 entry whose test is not in `all`: that entry takes no credit (<<sep-vplan-credit-rules>>)
|Which tests count |The `all` group, pinned by its `expected_count` in `testlists/all.toml` |`smoke`, `cpu_stub` and `cpu` are subsets of `all`. `rom_fw` and `rom_fw_smoke` are outside this plan (<<sep-vplan-scope>>)
|Coverage intent per requirement |`SEP_FCOV.adoc` and `cov/sv/sep_fcov.sv` |The matrix names the covergroups that a row leans on. The bin definitions stay in `SEP_FCOV.adoc`
|Code-coverage points that cannot be hit |The exclusion lists and the scope file under `cov/config/vcs/` |`SEP_FCOV.adoc` (<<sep-fcov-closure>>) states the RTL fact behind each list. This matrix repeats none of them
|===

[[sep-vplan-live-groups]]
==== Live groups versus credit

Executable membership comes from the testlists. The `expected_count` guard of
`all` pins its size.

[cols="1,4",options="header"]
|===
|Group |Role
|`smoke` |Pull-request gate (`sim.yml`): `sep_axi_smoke_test`. Bring-up, not a credit set
|`cpu_stub` |Runtime class: the `no_cpu` members of `all`. A partition of `all`, not a credit set of its own
|`cpu` |Runtime class: the firmware-boot members of `all`. A partition of `all`, not a credit set of its own
|`all` |The credit denominator: every test that this plan grades, the union of `cpu_stub` and `cpu`
|`rom_fw`, `rom_fw_smoke` |Outside this plan. No credit. <<sep-rom-dv-verification-plan,SEP_ROM_VPLAN>> is their plan
|===

[[sep-vplan-credit-rules]]
==== Credit rules

* Feature credit requires a test in `all`, the named evidence that its entry
  declares (`CHK-<ID> PASS` lines, <<sep-vplan-evidence-gate>>), and a positive
  `results.xml`. A failing test gives no credit.
* A row names the tests whose checkers prove that feature, and states in the
  row any part of the feature those tests leave unproven. Feature rows are
  independent of per-test checkers.
* The architecture specification under `hw/sys/sep/doc/` is the denominator: a
  feature that the specification does not define is not a row. An *iconic* row
  is the mode, command, or contract that makes that IP itself.
* A feature takes credit once, at the earliest phase that proves it. A later
  test that also reaches the feature does not take the credit again.
* A known RTL defect or specification gap that the suite exercises is a
  <<known-limitations>> row, not a feature credit.
* The cocotb and SV-UVM realizations of one name are one scenario and take
  credit once (<<sv-uvm-smoke>>).
* Coverage credit is the covergroup bins of `cov/sv/sep_fcov.sv`, collected on
  VCS. A bin records that an interface event happened, never that it was
  correct. Correctness stays with the checkers.
* A Phase 3 entry (<<sep-vplan-phase3>>) takes credit only when its test is in
  `all`, the named evidence that its entry declares (`CHK-<ID> PASS` lines) is
  in the leaf log, and `results.xml` is positive.
* [[sep-vplan-reserved-bits]]Every graded register compare of a Phase 3 entry
  (an expected value from the RDL or a model, a read-back, a before and after
  compare, or a compare of two reads) uses the RDL field bits of the register
  only. A reserved bit is graded only where the RDL or the specification states
  its value. A reserved bit with no stated value is masked out of the compare,
  and the PASS or FAIL line logs the masked bits and the mask.
* The Phase 3 entries, the Phase 3 lists and `SEP_FCOV.adoc` use four class
  words and four terms for bench items, with these meanings:
** *PROPOSED*: a hook that drives, forces or deposits into a net or a state
   inside the `sep` module for a purpose other than error injection, and that
   is not in `hw/sys/sep/dv/tb/tb_top.sv` and not in the closed set of
   `docs/SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc` (<<sep-vplan-phase3-proposed>>).
** *Observation tap*: a read-only observation that drives nothing: a testbench
   variable, an XMR probe port, an observation `bind` or a hierarchical read
   reference of a DUT net. An observation tap is not PROPOSED and blocks no
   check, leg or bin. A tap that `docs/SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc` does not list is an
   observation tap the test adds to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list
   (<<sep-vplan-phase3-proposed>>).
** *Added hook*: an error inject or a top-port drive that `docs/SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`
   does not list and that `hw/sys/sep/dv/tb/tb_top.sv` does not have. An error
   inject is an inject or a force that puts a fault or
   an illegal condition into the DUT, at a net inside the `sep` module or
   outside it. A top-port drive is a drive of a port of the `sep` module by the
   bench: a stream input, a strap, an SMC, JTAG, DMI, DFT or debug port, a
   memory-macro response port, or a port value that a plusarg sets. An added
   hook is not PROPOSED and blocks no check, leg or bin. The test adds an error
   inject to the inject exceptions of `docs/SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc` and a top-port
   drive to the port table of its HDL Top section (<<sep-vplan-phase3-proposed>>).
** *Existing bench hook*: a drive, a force, an inject or a time-0 plusarg that
   `hw/sys/sep/dv/tb/tb_top.sv` has. It is existing DV infrastructure and is
   approved, whatever net it drives and whatever its purpose: it is not
   PROPOSED, it is not an added hook, and it blocks no check, leg or bin. When
   `docs/SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc` does not list it, the test that uses it adds its row
   to `docs/SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`, and <<sep-vplan-phase3-added-hooks>> names it with
   the kind "Existing bench hook".
** *Preload*: the content of a memory macro outside `sep` that the approved
   image load `tb_backdoor_mem` of `hw/sys/sep/dv/tb/tb_top.sv` puts in place at
   t=0, before the macro is read: a Key Manager ROM image, its parameter block or
   a `rom_main` build, loaded through `+km_rom_hex`. A preload is part of the
   approved t=0 image load. It is not a hook, it is not PROPOSED, and it blocks
   no check, leg or bin. A test that uses a new `rom_main` build adds the build
   to the section "Key Manager `rom_main` Firmware Builds" of
   `docs/SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc` (<<sep-vplan-km-proposed>>).
** *Blocked*: a check, leg or coverage bin that needs a PROPOSED item, or a
   value that the specification or the register description does not state
   (<<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked>>). An entry writes `Blocked: <item or value>`
   at the check, leg or bin: "Blocked: needs the PROPOSED drive `x`" at a check,
   leg or bin, or "Blocked: the specification does not state X". A blocked feature is a feature whose checks all need such
   a value. A blocked check, leg or bin takes no credit and is not in an
   executable total.
** *Executable*: a Phase 3 test with at least one graded check that is not
   blocked. A coverage bin is executable when it is not blocked and an
   executable check of an executable owning test fills it. A coverpoint or
   group is executable when every bin is executable, and *executable in part*
   when some bins are executable and some are blocked.
** *Parked*: a Phase 3 test with no executable check
   (<<sep-vplan-phase3-parked>>), or a Phase 2 coverpoint or group with no
   executable bin (<<sep-fcov-parked,SEP_FCOV>>). The entry of a parked test
   has the field `*Needs*: <PROPOSED items or unstated values>`. A parked test
   takes no credit and is not in an executable total. A coverpoint with no
   executable bin is written "parked: <item or value>", for example "parked:
   needs the PROPOSED drive `x`".
* The behaviours of <<sep-vplan-scope>> take no credit.

[[iconic-scorecard]]
==== Matrix

One row per iconic feature. *Tests* links each owning test to its entry.
*Groups* lists the testlist groups of those tests. *Coverage* names the
Phase 1 covergroups that list a test of the row as an owner
(<<sep-fcov-owners>>); "None" means that no test of the row owns a Phase 1
bin. *Checker evidence* states what the checkers of those tests prove and what
they leave out; the checker table of each linked entry is the full set.

|Per-IP `SW_RESET_N` isolation |<<sep_crypto_per_ip_reset_isolation_test,`sep_crypto_per_ip_reset_isolation_test`>> |all, cpu_stub |`sep_crypto_isolate_cg` |Each engine holds a golden-checked result; a neighbour reset leaves it bit-exact. While HMAC is held, the same `DIGEST_0` read and a `CFG` write complete SLVERR, the read does not return the live digest, and AES stays OKAY. A software reset of OTBN or AES drops that engine's outstanding `edn_req` with no `edn_ack` (CHK-EDN-FLUSH, CHK-EDN-FLUSH-AES), a KMAC reset leaves a pending AES request high and unacknowledged (CHK-EDN-FLUSH-KMAC), and a sibling client is still granted afterwards. The cancel of the KMAC endpoint's own request is logged only (OBS-EDN-NOACK-WINDOW, INFO-EDN-EDGE-KMAC-IDLE). A word committed and not yet acknowledged when the cancel starts is never acknowledged to any client afterwards, and a KMAC reset stepped across one URND acknowledge period leaves URND undisturbed while it completes its own beat (CHK-EDN-EDGE); the KMAC endpoint on those edges is usually idle, which is logged (INFO-EDN-EDGE-KMAC-IDLE). The `edn_bus` of a cancelled endpoint has no stated value and is logged only (OBS-EDN-CANCEL-BUS). A read accepted after the isolate request rose completes SLVERR with read data `0x1501A7ED` (CHK-DRAIN-ARRIVAL, CHK-KMAC-DRAIN-ARRIVAL, CHK-ABR-DRAIN-ARRIVAL). Drain-before-reset is claimed for the HMAC, KMAC and Adams Bridge domains — see the detail entry; it is not claimed for AES or OTBN. The ABR domain also carries the JTAG `IC_RESET` override check, and its live-result behaviour is graded on `sep_abr_mlkem_kat_test`. Drain-before-reset for the shared TRNG domain is on the `Coordinated TRNG reset and ordered recovery` row. A software reset of AES, KMAC and OTBN returns `CTRL_SHADOWED`, the KMAC `STATUS` fields and `LOAD_CHECKSUM` to their reset values after a read before the pulse shows the programmed value (CHK-SELF-RESET, CHK-KMAC-SELF, CHK-OTBN). The KMAC `STATE` reads 0 in both shares after KMAC's own pulse (CHK-KMAC-STATE). The reset value of the HMAC `DIGEST` is not stated and is logged only (OBS-HMAC-DIGEST). KMAC, AES, OTBN URND and OTBN RND are each reset with the request live at the cancel; the acknowledges from the isolated cycle to the release are logged only (OBS-EDN-NOACK-WINDOW), because the stimulus places no due acknowledge in that window, and each KMAC trial grades the drain order (CHK-KMAC-DRAIN-ORDER). |YES for the claimed part; the evidence cell names the rest
|Scratch bank reset domains (warm vs cold) |<<sep_warm_cold_reset_scratch_test,`sep_warm_cold_reset_scratch_test`>> |all, cpu_stub |`sep_scratch_reset_cg` |The graded contract is the reset-domain split across every implemented register: a warm reset clears all eight warm registers while all eight cold registers retain, and only a cold reset clears both banks. Distinct per-index patterns prove the registers do not alias. Read over the CPU-LSU AXI splice with a `scratch_cold_probe_o` cross-check; the cross-check catches a read-mux fault and is not an independent confirmation, since both sides come from the same flops. |YES
|Coordinated TRNG reset and ordered recovery |<<sep_trng_reset_recovery_test,`sep_trng_reset_recovery_test`>> |all, cpu_stub |None |One `SW_RESET_N` bit resets ESRC, CSRNG and EDN together, and the reset waits for all three converted AXI-Lite paths to report isolated, so drain-before-reset for this domain is covered here. Also covers the two-stage synchronization of the reset-derived downstream clear, pool scrub, SLVERR while isolated, the external source-select register staying outside the domain, and the JTAG hold/release pair. A read refused while a path is isolated carries the data `0x1501A7ED`, and ESRC gives no seed between the clear of the JTAG override and the generator enable (CHK-TRNG-NO-EARLY-SEED). Intra-window decode of the adopter TRNG passthrough is not graded. |YES for the claimed part; the evidence cell names the rest

The two read-lock domains are graded apart: an eFuse read lock withholds the shadow read and leaves both KM views, and `OTP_READ_LOCK` zeroes the KMCSR words and leaves the input. With `secure_tm` at 1 (strap 1) the 48 KMCSR words still equal the staged image, and the four secrets `CHIPLET_UID`, `SIP_UID`, `SYS_UID` and `CLASS_KEY` read valid zero in KMCSR (every value word 0, every complement word all ones); at strap 0 they read the staged secrets. The KM input, `OTP_LIFE_CYCLE` and `OTP_DEMOTION_STATE` at strap 1, `OTP_READ_LOCK_COLD` for these fields, and identity-key derivation from them are not claimed. |YES for the claimed part; the evidence cell names the rest
|SEC_DIS override |<<sep_sec_dis_override_test,`sep_sec_dis_override_test`>> |all, cpu_stub |`sep_feat_ctrl_cg`, `sep_sec_dis_boot_cg`, `sep_sec_dis_robustness_cg` |`CHK-OVR-ALONE` grades the `sep_reset_n` override before any match: reset releases and feature control stays closed (`hw/sys/sep/doc/security_disable.adoc`). The SEP core is held in reset until sensing completes, so while sense is stopped the OTP JTAG port presents the tokens and makes the retained-window eFuse accesses, and each frontdoor read runs inside an override release. `CHK-SENSE-GATED-RESET` grades that the JTAG match leaves the reset held. `CHK-FEAT-PRESENSE` grades that the match opens feature control before sensing finishes (`hw/sys/sep/doc/token_processing.adoc`). `CHK-OVR-AFTER` grades the same override inputs after the match. `CHK-MAP-OPEN` requires an OKAY `LC_STATE` shadow-map read while sensing is still open. `CHK-SENSE-STUCK` grades that the match stops the sense. The `CHK-RETAIN-*` checkers grade the recovery reset: the token digest survives `rst_ni`, so SEC_DIS, the open map and the override still work and sense stays stopped. `CHK-DROP` and `CHK-SENSE-CONTROL` clear SEC_DIS and grade a full sense after a second reset. In the retained SEC_DIS window (sense stopped, every shadow at its reset value), `OBS-SECDIS-SHADOW-WR` logs the response and the JTAG readback of a JTAG write of a 0 bit of `REQUIRED_SIGNERS`, without a verdict, because no line states a shadow write before sense under SEC_DIS (`hw/sys/sep/doc/otp_fuse_controller.adoc:66`, `:121`, `:325` and `hw/sys/sep/regs/blocks/sep_efuse_map/sep_efuse_map.rdl:846-851` state it after sense); `CTRL-SECDIS-SHADOW-WR` requires the reset value before the write. `CTRL-SECDIS-JTAG-REACH` requires each JTAG read of that window to answer AXI OKAY (`hw/sys/sep/doc/otp_fuse_controller.adoc`, Shadow Register and JTAG Access Control: the held INVALID state does not restrict JTAG), so a refused or dead port fails. `CHK-SECDIS-LC-WR` grades that a JTAG write of the PROD encoding to the INVALID `LC_STATE` word leaves its JTAG readback at its reset word `0x0000000f` (`hw/sys/sep/regs/blocks/sep_efuse_map/sep_efuse_map.rdl:573-577`; `hw/sys/sep/doc/lifecycle_controller.adoc:87`, `:90`; `hw/sys/sep/doc/otp_fuse_controller.adoc:220-224`: INVALID is terminal), and `OBS-SECDIS-LC-WR` logs the write response without a verdict. `CHK-RETAIN-LC-RESET` grades that the `rst_ni` pulse under SEC_DIS returns the `LC_STATE` word from its sensed value to its reset value `0x0F` (`hw/sys/sep/doc/security_disable.adoc`, Reset Persistence). `OBS-SECDIS-DBG-BUS` logs the bits of `ext_debug_bus_o` that change while those two writes run and in a quiet window before and after them, without a verdict: no specification puts eFuse state on the bus. The JTAG write response is logged and not graded, because the specification states no response for a write that the terminal lifecycle rule refuses. After the later full sense, `CHK-OVERRIDE-TM0` grades that a fresh match with the `TEST_EN` strap at 0 gives an all-ones `FEAT_CTRL` that equals `lcc_feat_ctrl_probe_o`, with `secure_tm_o` at 0 (`hw/sys/sep/doc/lifecycle_controller.adoc:336`, `:341`; `hw/sys/sep/doc/security_disable.adoc:25`; `hw/sys/sep/doc/test_mode.adoc:12`, `:34`). The post-sense match, mismatch and restore cells also run in this leaf. The leaf drives the SEP override inputs and reads the reset probes. `sep_sec_dis_reset_reach_test` grades the bus access and the IC_RESET TDR write. |YES

SW-writable rows, `REQUIRED_SIGNERS` included, overwrite and then a write of 0 clears. |YES
|eFuse per-spare write-lock slot numbering |<<sep_efuse_program_lock_matrix_test,`sep_efuse_program_lock_matrix_test`>> |all, cpu_stub |`sep_efuse_program_lock_cg` |Every run walks `SPARE0..SPARE8`: each spare is programmed while unlocked, then its own `LOCKS_SPARE` bit is set and a second bit in the same spare is rejected. Slot coverage does not depend on the seed; only the bit offsets within a spare do. |YES

A redundancy fault forces the reserved error code onto the token status, and the `LC_STATE` bit[1] advance must not land — the gate compares for equality with the match code, so a gate written as "not a mismatch" would let the fault code through. |Dead-space decode |<<sep_fabric_deadspace_decode_test,`sep_fabric_deadspace_decode_test`>> |all, cpu_stub |None |Single-beat refuse and no-alias in every block window, and the crypto-region burst rule. `CHK-DEADSPACE-BEAT` reads each beat's `RRESP` off the bus and requires DECERR on every beat of a read burst into the crypto region. `CHK-DEADSPACE-BURST` requires the write burst there to answer DECERR and move no register, and no read beat to return a live value. With a bench endpoint on the TRNG port, `CHK-TRNG-REPLY` requires a single-beat TRNG-window read or write to return the endpoint's OKAY, SLVERR or DECERR unchanged, `CHK-TRNG-SPLIT-WR` requires a split 64-bit write with an earlier error and a last OKAY to return SLVERR, and `CHK-TRNG-BURST` requires a burst to answer DECERR on every beat with no beat at the port. Bursts into other register regions are outside the specification and are not graded; see <<known-limitations>>. `CHK-TRNG-REPLY` also requires the port address to carry the issued window offset (first, last and an interior word forced), and `CHK-TRNG-UNOWNED` grades the read word `0xBADCAB1E` of the window with no TRNG fitted. |YES for the claimed part; the evidence cell names the rest

|Interrupt aggregator packing under simultaneous fan-in |<<sep_irq_simultaneous_fanin_no_alias_test,`sep_irq_simultaneous_fanin_no_alias_test`>> |all, cpu_stub |None |Several cross-IP sources asserted at once must produce exactly their own bits in `sep_internal_interrupts[8:33]`, with every bit of that region resolved rather than floating. `PIC source map` grades the source-to-PIC-id map and CPU delivery on `sep_pic_irq_source_map_delivery_test`; this row is the aggregation itself — the place a one-hot map can smear onto a neighbour — and is a distinct contract from delivery. |YES
|SPI host control plane (CSR, interrupt, error status, watermark, enable) |<<sep_spi_ot_host_csr_irq_rand_test,`sep_spi_ot_host_csr_irq_rand_test`>> |all, cpu_stub |`sep_spi_flash_cg`, `sep_spi_host_rand_cg` |Documented resets, per-register write/readback with unimplemented bits proven to hold no storage, the OpenTitan Event-versus-Status interrupt contract (`INTR_STATE.ERROR` latches and clears only on write-one-to-clear; `INTR_STATE.SPI_EVENT` is set by the `INTR_TEST` force and, with EVENT_ENABLE 0, returns to 0 with PIC source 14 when `INTR_TEST` is written back to 0, because it is a level-sensitive status of the EVENT_ENABLE conditions: `spi.adoc:84-86`, `sep-programming.adoc:819-820`), `INTR_ENABLE` gating of `sep_internal_interrupts[13]`, five of the six `ERROR_STATUS` conditions each induced by its own trigger and exclusive of the others (ACCESSINVAL is not claimed as a trigger: its documented trigger is a write with no bytes enabled, which neither this AXI master nor the wrapper bridge presents to the core; the extension grades that an 8-byte write with an empty TXDATA half leaves it at 0, CHK-SPI-STRB-EMPTY), a 64-bit CONTROL write whose empty STATUS half the wrapper must ack, the transmit watermark polarity at the exact threshold, and the enable gate against a calibrated negative window. `SPI-OT flash command set` grades the flash datapath and `CSR reset / RW / RO / reserved` is the generic bit-bash vehicle; neither covers this control plane. |YES for the claimed part; the evidence cell names the rest
|SECURE_TM shadow-write lock |<<sep_efuse_secure_tm_write_lock_test,`sep_efuse_secure_tm_write_lock_test`>> |all, cpu_stub |None |Each of `LOCKS`, `LOCKS_SPARE`, `LC_STATE`, `SIP_DIS` and `SYS_DIS` takes the same seeded payload twice: with the strap low the write lands (set-only rows OR-merge; `LC_STATE[31:8]` takes a plain shadow write), and after the strap is raised and the image re-sensed the stored value does not move: the bus readback equals the read before the write and the sensed shadow word on `efuse_shadow_probe_o` equals the staged OTP word. `CTRL-SECTM-LATCH` logs `secure_tm_o` after each sense (0 before the strap-low half, 1 before the deny half). The write response and the `sep_internal_interrupts[33]` level of the refused write, and of the contrast write after it, are logged, not graded (`OBS-SECTM-IRQ`: no specification sentence ties a SECURE_TM refusal to source 34). A `SecureTmUnlock` spare still takes a write with the strap up, so the refusal is the lock rather than a dead write path. Real fuse sense at `LC_PROD`. |YES for the claimed part; the evidence cell names the rest
|eFuse locked-field access violation interrupt |<<sep_locked_field_access_irq_path_test,`sep_locked_field_access_irq_path_test`>> |all, cpu_stub |None |A write to a write-locked spare and a read of a read-locked spare each raise `sep_internal_interrupts[33]` (PIC source 34) during the access. Each locked access is the first one after a SEP reset, with `[33]` low before it (`CTRL-IRQ-IDLE`). The `[33]` level after a locked access is logged, not graded (`OBS-LF-IRQ-DEASSERT`): no specification sentence states it. The write-locked spare keeps its pre-write value, and the read-locked read does not return the stored value, which the leaf reads from the sensed shadow (`efuse_shadow_probe_o`) and requires to equal the staged pattern first (`CTRL-READ-STAGED`). An unlocked spare in the same OTP image is the negative control. The response code of a locked access is logged, not graded. Real fuse sense at `LC_PROD`. No other vector bit moves at the rise of `[33]` (`CHK-SRC34-EXCL`). |YES for the claimed part; the evidence cell names the rest
|Token comparator redundancy fault delivery to the CPU |<<sep_efuse_token_match_fault_pic_test,`sep_efuse_token_match_fault_pic_test`>> |all, cpu |None |EL2 claims PIC source 40, reads the sticky `TOKEN_MATCH_FAULT` bit, and masks `meie[40]`; because the fault bits are software-read-only and clear only on reset, the check requires the source to be still requesting across the quiet window, which is what makes the mask the reason for the quiet. `RMA token match -> LC update` grades the comparator matrix on `sep_efuse_rma_token_rand_test` and is not re-credited here. |YES
|===

=== Scenario-Expanded Test Summary

One table per area, in the order of the Test Procedures. *Phase* is the scope
tier of the Verification Strategy. A Phase 3 row that reads "3, parked" names
a parked test (<<sep-vplan-phase3-parked>>). Each test name links to its entry. The
SV-UVM realization of a scenario keeps the cocotb name (<<sv-uvm-smoke>>).

|Two crypto sinks draw EDN entropy concurrently and both complete correctly: each AES fork block matches its golden bit-exact and the KMAC digest matches its own, while both are shown to have taken real post-adapter EDN beats inside the contention window. An AES-alone baseline in which KMAC takes zero beats is the control that keeps the both-beats check from passing vacuously.
|<<sep_crypto_edn_round_robin_grant_test,`sep_crypto_edn_round_robin_grant_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense, +esrc_noise_force
|ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden
|Holds AES and OTBN URND `edn_req` together, then grades that both clients are granted. Round-robin order under simultaneous request is not claimed: the round-robin arbiter fills a one-entry FIFO per client, so the `edn_ack` order at the client ports is not the arbitration order and is not stated, and a fixed-priority arbiter is not excluded. Then drives OTBN RND with a program blocked on the RND CSR and KMAC with a keyed digest, so all four adapter clients are live, value-checked consumers in one run, with the entropy pool scored alongside them as a fifth endpoint. The first post-adapter grants and the length of their alternating prefix are logged, not graded (`OBS-EDN-GRANT-SEQ`).
|<<sep_drbg_gen_segmentation_test,`sep_drbg_gen_segmentation_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden
|Phase 2 DRBG iconic feature: a CSRNG Generate command completes at the commanded length and `gen_last` asserts. The trailing CTR_DRBG Update is not observed on this vehicle.
|<<sep_crypto_per_ip_reset_isolation_test,`sep_crypto_per_ip_reset_isolation_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense, +esrc_noise_force
|ocah_axi_vip
|Proves the per-IP software reset domains are isolated, using live crypto results rather than a poked status bit: each engine computes a golden-checked value, then one engine is reset and the other's held result must survive bit-exact. A software reset of AES returns `CTRL_SHADOWED` to the reset value of each field, compared on the field mask, after a read before the pulse shows the programmed value (CHK-SELF-RESET; `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/aes/data/aes.hjson:627`, `:650`, `:675`, `:730`, `:762`, `:770`, `:804`). KMAC `STATUS` (the fields with a stated reset value: `sha3_idle`, `fifo_empty` and the two alert bits, `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/kmac/data/kmac.hjson:707`, `:737`, `:745`, `:759`) and OTBN `LOAD_CHECKSUM` follow the same rule (CHK-KMAC-SELF, CHK-OTBN). An isolated HMAC read returns SLVERR with data `0x1501A7ED` (CHK-ISOLATE-SLVERR; `hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc:139-141`). The KMAC `STATE` reads 0 in both shares after KMAC's own pulse while idle (CHK-KMAC-STATE; `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/kmac/doc/theory_of_operation.md`, "Keccak State Access"). The reset values of the HMAC `DIGEST` and of the KMAC `STATUS` fields `sha3_absorb`, `sha3_squeeze`, `fifo_depth` and `fifo_full` have no stated value and are logged only (OBS-HMAC-DIGEST, OBS-HMAC-DIGEST-REOPEN, OBS-KMAC-STATUS-UNSTATED). KMAC, AES, OTBN URND and OTBN RND resets that find the endpoint's request live log the acknowledges from the isolated cycle to the release (OBS-EDN-NOACK-WINDOW; `hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc:161-165`), because the stimulus places no due acknowledge in that window; the `edn_bus` of a cancelled endpoint is logged only (OBS-EDN-CANCEL-BUS). The discard is graded only on words committed and not yet acknowledged at a cancel start, at least one required (CHK-EDN-DISCARD); a word held at the reset request is logged only (OBS-EDN-HELD-AT-REQUEST). Each read accepted after an isolate request rose (HMAC, KMAC, Adams Bridge) completes SLVERR with read data `0x1501A7ED` (CHK-DRAIN-ARRIVAL, CHK-KMAC-DRAIN-ARRIVAL, CHK-ABR-DRAIN-ARRIVAL; `hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc:139-141`). Each graded KMAC trial grades the drain order (CHK-KMAC-DRAIN-ORDER; `hw/sys/sep/doc/crypto.adoc:97-98`). `FIPS_LOCK.LOCK` set before a TRNG-only reset reads its reset value after the release (CHK-TRNG-FIPS-LOCK-RESET; `hw/ip/entropy_source/doc/architecture.adoc:224-227`). A read of ESRC, CSRNG and EDN inside that reset completes SLVERR with read data `0x1501A7ED` (CHK-TRNG-ISO-RESP; `hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc:139-141`, `:155`).
|<<sep_trng_reset_recovery_test,`sep_trng_reset_recovery_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense, +esrc_noise_force
|ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden
|The counterpart for the one reset shared by ESRC, CSRNG and EDN: the pool is filled from the internal chain, every consumer is held, and the reset must wait for all three converted AXI-Lite paths to isolate before it asserts. Proves the downstream clear crosses into `clk_i` through two synchronizer stages, the pool scrubs its stale and half-packed data, the isolated paths answer SLVERR while the external source-select register stays outside the domain, and recovery is fresh-only and ordered — consumers are released only after fresh pool progress. A read that is outstanding at the isolation either completes OKAY or is refused with SLVERR and the data `0x1501A7ED`, and at least one completes OKAY (CHK-TRNG-DRAIN); a new read of an isolated ESRC, CSRNG or EDN path returns SLVERR with the data `0x1501A7ED` (CHK-TRNG-SLVERR; `hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc:127-130`). ESRC presents no seed between the clear of the JTAG override and the generator enable, and presents one after it (CHK-TRNG-NO-EARLY-SEED; `hw/sys/sep/doc/trng.adoc:198-199`). The coverage bin `cp_trng_drain[drained_slverr]` is seed-dependent: it needs a drain read that the isolate refuses, and the seed draws the issue slot of the CSRNG and EDN reads.
|<<sep_abr_mldsa_keygen_kat_test,`sep_abr_mldsa_keygen_kat_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|ML-DSA-87 key generation through the ABR register aperture: reset status, an ACVP keyGen known-answer public key, completion status, the PIC error and notification legs, and `ZEROIZE`. A second keyGen on the ACVP seed with one bit flipped is logged, not graded (`OBS-ABR-KEYGEN-SENSITIVITY`: no golden for the flipped seed). The PIC legs are split into edge checks (`CHK-PIC-ERROR-RISE`, `CHK-PIC-ERROR-W1C`, `CHK-PIC-NOTIF-RISE`, `CHK-PIC-NOTIF-W1C`) and status checks (`CHK-PIC-ERROR-STS`, `CHK-PIC-NOTIF-STS`), with `CHK-SRC35-EXCL` and `CHK-SRC36-EXCL` at the two rises, in owner window 753 (`cp_abr_pic`, `cp_src_exclusive_other[s35, s36]`; each `cp_abr_pic` edge samples on the `abr_pic_pass` label after its edge check passed, and each `cp_src_exclusive_other` rise on the `exo_pass` label after its EXCL check passed). The `NAME` and `VERSION` words are logged, not graded (`OBS-ABR-MLDSA-ID`).
|<<sep_abr_mldsa_sign_verify_kat_test,`sep_abr_mldsa_sign_verify_kat_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|ML-DSA-87 signing and verification through the same aperture: a deterministic external-mu signature compared word-for-word against the ACVP sigGen vector, and the sigVer pair that must verify and must not, with `MLDSA_VERIFY_RES` compared against each signature's c~.
|<<sep_abr_mldsa_hint_reject_kat_test,`sep_abr_mldsa_hint_reject_kat_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|ML-DSA-87 verification of the NIST signatures with a malformed hint encoding, pure with a context and with external mu: each is rejected, with `MLDSA_VERIFY_RES` different from c~, and a readback of the hint words of `MLDSA_SIGNATURE` and of `MLDSA_PUBKEY` after VALID proves the malformed hint and the key were written (CTRL-HINT-SIG-LOADED, CTRL-HINT-PK-LOADED). A pure-with-context case and an external-mu case that must verify are the controls (CHK-HINT-CONTROL). On the pure cases `MLDSA_VERIFY_RES` reads ~c~ right after VERIFYING is accepted (CHK-HINT-PRELOAD; `hw/sys/sep/bootrom/prod/doc/abr-mldsa-driver.adoc:70-72`).
|<<sep_abr_zeroize_latency_test,`sep_abr_zeroize_latency_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip, `abr_zeroize_ready_probe_o`
|ML-DSA ZEROIZE from idle, mid-stream and VALID: STATUS returns to READY alone, READY reads clear at the VALID park, and the engine-side and bus-side zeroize latency is logged.
|<<sep_abr_mldsa87_verify_test,`sep_abr_mldsa87_verify_test`>>
|2
|cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip, CPU FW
|ML-DSA-87 verification driven by SEP firmware through the boot ROM's ABR driver: a streamed message from committed vectors signed with the test-only key verifies, and each rejected variant fails closed with the accelerator's own result recorded.
|<<sep_abr_cmd_drop_test,`sep_abr_cmd_drop_test`>>
|2
|cpu, +skip_fuse_sense, +sep_abr_cmd_drop
|ocah_axi_vip, CPU FW
|Fault injection on the ML-DSA command: the accelerator never runs, and the verify driver must refuse at its preload check rather than trust whatever `MLDSA_VERIFY_RES` holds, and the firmware must complete with FAIL.
|<<sep_abr_pipelined_read_test,`sep_abr_pipelined_read_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|ABR is the one crypto block reached over AHB, through `axi_lite_to_ahb`. An SoC master may hold several single-beat reads outstanding with distinct `ARID`s, and each must be answered with its own register's value. Every read in the sweep targets the SAME read-only register, so every response must carry the identical value and no reordering or per-id confusion can explain a difference. A bridge that drops `HADDR[2:0]` on streamed reads answers zero with an OKAY response from the third read in flight.
|<<sep_abr_mlkem_kat_test,`sep_abr_mlkem_kat_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|ML-KEM-1024 key generation, encapsulation and decapsulation through the ML-KEM register block: the two keys, the ciphertext and the shared key each compared against the published ACVP vector, with decapsulation walked as both the valid case and the modified-ciphertext case that FIPS-203 answers by implicit rejection. Also the Adams Bridge `SW_RESET_N` domain: a live encaps result survives a sibling engine's reset bit-exact, the host path returns SLVERR while the ABR bit is held, and the result does not survive ABR's own reset. The `NAME` and `VERSION` words are logged, not graded (`OBS-ABR-MLKEM-ID`).
|<<sep_abr_pipelined_read_matrix_test,`sep_abr_pipelined_read_matrix_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu + external SMN master, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|Several reads in flight on the ABR aperture each return the word they name: concurrent single-beat reads of `MLDSA_VERSION1` at depths 1, 2, 3, 4 and 8 on each TB master, the four ML-DSA identity words in several orders and on one ARID, and both masters at once. The AR and R handshakes recorded on the pins prove the reads overlapped, and every R beat is graded by the RID it carried.
|<<sep_abr_access_size_test,`sep_abr_access_size_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|The ABR access-size rules of `adams_bridge.adoc`: a 64-bit read returns both registers it covers, a narrow read the addressed bytes with every other byte lane zero, a 64-bit write lands exactly the words its strobe covers whole, and every partial-word strobe answers SLVERR without moving the register. Past the register map (`0xC018`) the crypto demux answers every word read, word write and partial-word write with DECERR, and no live register moves (`hw/sys/sep/doc/adams_bridge.adoc`, aperture-decode paragraph). Inside the register map, a read, an all-ones write and a zero write at the upper neighbour of the counter and at one seeded word that no register or memory window owns return no bus error: the response codes and the read data are the "Hole in Extent" cell of the ABR row of the generated SEP memory map, the written value is not stored, and no live register moves (CHK-ABR-INMAP-HOLE; `adams_bridge.adoc:107-108` for the response class, `hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/memory_map.adoc:66` for the codes and the data, generated from the default hole response `ocah_hole_resp` at `hw/sys/sep/regs/include/sep_memory_map.rdl`). A mismatch with the specification is a specification question.
|<<sep_abr_concurrent_rw_test,`sep_abr_concurrent_rw_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu + external SMN master, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|Seeded interleaved reads and writes from both TB masters at once, the first half of each stream with one access outstanding on each master and the second half with a seeded number from 3 to 6 on each, and both response channels throttled, graded by a scoreboard that bounds each read by its register owner's write window and checks each register's final value. The six `x_abr_concurrent` cells are written to the pass label only after CHK-ABR-CRW-WRITE-RESP, CHK-ABR-CRW-READ and CHK-ABR-CRW-STRESS pass.
|<<sep_abr_absent_decerr_test,`sep_abr_absent_decerr_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense, `lsu_stub_abr_absent` model, Verilator only
|ocah_axi_vip, KM CPU image
|The build without Adams Bridge (`SEP_ABR_EN` not defined): host reads and writes of the ABR aperture answer DECERR, and KM CPU stores and loads of the ABR wrapper key aperture set AXI_DECERR, with HMAC controls on both paths answering clean.
|<<sep_entropy_pool_aperture_test,`sep_entropy_pool_aperture_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden
|Every seed walks the high-bit mirrors of the three live registers, refuses writes to each live offset and one unmapped offset, exercises all three AW/W arrival orders on the shared write path, and refuses a 32-bit read of each register's upper word without popping or disturbing the FIFO. Between two `STATUS` reads, a `DATA` pop lowers `fifo_level` by exactly one; a `STATUS` read, an `IRQ_CAUSE` read, a refused write and a refused upper-word `DATA` read leave it unchanged; and no pool EDN beat falls in the pair (CHK-POOL-LEVEL-STEP; `hw/sys/sep/doc/entropy_pool.adoc:31`, `:34`, `:38-39`, `:42-43`). One added `STATUS` read after the stall-phase `IRQ_CAUSE` reads grades the kept level, and each of the five classes (pop, status, cause, write, upper `DATA`) must have at least one graded pair. The `fifo_level` at which `pool_low` changes is logged, not graded (`OBS-POOL-LOW-LEVEL`).

The `cp_capability` bin of each program samples only after its check passes. The stimulus self-report of the seed is logged as `INFO-RANDCFG` and is not graded.

A supported selector reads back as written before the watermark read (`sw = rw`, `hw/ip/entropy_source/regs/entropy_source.rdl:802`), so a write that did not reach the DUT fails (CHK-NO-REARM; `entropy_source.rdl:800`). The entry arm of the opposite polarity is graded like the leg arm (CHK-ARM-MEN; `entropy_source.rdl:811`), and a low mode first arms `0xFFFF` and then tracks a lower value (CHK-LOW-FALL). |<<sep_esrc_fips_lock_test,`sep_esrc_fips_lock_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden
|The FIPS lock makes ESRC configuration write-once: a pre-lock write moves the field off reset, the same field rejects a poke after `LOCK=1`, write-0 cannot clear the lock, and `rst_ni` clears it (the TRNG reset also does, `hw/ip/entropy_source/doc/architecture.adoc:227`, graded by `sep_trng_reset_recovery_test`). The lock also freezes the debug-pin mux and both observe-tap enables: one tap held at 1 rejects a clear, the other held at 0 rejects a set, while `NOISE_OBS_CTRL.LANE_SEL` stays writable. `FIFO_CTRL.ENABLE`, cleared before the lock, rejects a set under it, so software cannot re-open the `FIFO_RDATA` read path of the DRBG seed words. The advisory cutoffs derived from `MIN_ENTROPY_H` are graded over a sweep of Q4.4 values with one seeded point in [1.0, 8.0), once with no coverage window open and once inside it (CHK-REC-THRESH): `RCT_LIMIT` equals 1 + ceil(20 / H) for every H above 0 (`hw/ip/entropy_source/regs/entropy_source.rdl:966`), and `APT_LIMIT` equals the SP 800-90B 4.4.2 cutoff for the 1024-sample window with alpha = 2^-20, the alpha that SP 800-90B (January 2018) 4.4 uses for all the calculations of 4.4.1 and 4.4.2 and the alpha of the RCT formula (`entropy_source.rdl:956-957`, `:971`). The limits at H = 0 have no stated value and are logged only (OBS-REC-THRESH-H0). Before the lock, `CTRL` holds the written fields and ignores `RSVD0`; after the lock, the fields hold and `RSVD0` reads 0 (CHK-CTRL-RSVD). The lock and unlock writes must each complete with an OKAY B response (CHK-LOCK-SET, CHK-LOCK-W1S). Each write the lock refuses (target pokes, tap-enable writes, the post-lock `CTRL.RSVD0` write) must complete with a B response; no text states its code, so the code is logged only (OBS-POST-LOCK-RESP, OBS-ENABLE-LOCKED-RESP, OBS-CTRL-RSVD-LOCKED-RESP). `FIPS_LOCK.LOCK` reads 1 just before `rst_ni` and 0 after it (CHK-RST-NI; `hw/ip/entropy_source/regs/gen/adoc/entropy_source.adoc:752`). Scope: health-test *quality* is not claimed.

The status and alert latches are logged across the reset and not graded.

|<<sep_esrc_health_pattern_rand_test,`sep_esrc_health_pattern_rand_test`>>
|3
|no_cpu, `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+esrc_noise_force`
|CPU-LSU master, noise inject `esrc_noise_ext_i`, `esrc_decor_valid_o`, `esrc_decor_bytes_o`, `esrc_whiten_push_o`, `esrc_compress_vld_o`; SHA-256 status tap (observation tap)
|Proves, with exact-count noise patterns, that the adaptive-proportion health test trips on a biased generator lane while the repetition and Markov tests stay quiet under shared limits, that a limit beyond the exact run and alternation counts trips the repetition and Markov tests of every generator, that a constant-zero stream sets the adaptive-proportion and Markov low bits (the repetition bit is a control here; the high-side bit and counters are logged), that a constant non-zero stream from all lanes stuck at 1 trips the 32-bit adaptive-proportion high test, that enable and clear behave as specified, and that with whitening on the whitener turns a constant-zero stream into exactly 8 digest words per 16 input words. The repetition count after a disable and the whitener input after a post-boot health-test failure are logged as blocked. The five total-failure counters after each limit trial are logged only (`OBS-ESRC-HT-TOTALS`).

|<<sep_esrc_persistent_alert_rand_test,`sep_esrc_persistent_alert_rand_test`>>
|3
|no_cpu, `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+esrc_noise_force`
|CPU-LSU master, noise inject `esrc_noise_ext_i`, `esrc_compress_vld_o`; ESRC main state machine force (added hook, error inject, VCS only)
|Proves that exactly T failing windows trip the alert, that T - 1 do not, that a following clean window does not cancel the trip, that the alert halts the entropy output with the alert and persistent-failure status set, that threshold 0 disables it, and that the output stays halted until a MODULE_ENABLE cycle or a TRNG reset (the reset exit with no CTRL write after the reset). The trip latency is logged, not graded (<<known-limitations,Known Limitations>>). Each failing-window check is graded at the end of its own leg, after its control: the trips of a block run before its no-trip and consecutive legs, and threshold 0 runs after a trip block. A check releases its coverage only when its control was seen. CHK-PF-TRIP-HOLD is graded at the end of each trip. A leg after the trip legs grades the health-test counter integrity error (CHK-SM-ERR; `hw/ip/entropy_source/regs/gen/adoc/entropy_source.adoc:410`, `:140`, `:164`; `hw/ip/entropy_source/doc/architecture.adoc:136`): after a TRNG reset, an injected counter-copy mismatch of a drawn bit latches `MAIN_SM_STATUS.ERR` and, with all interrupts enabled, sets `INTR_STATUS.PERSISTENT_FAILURE`; a second mismatch of another bit followed by a TRNG reset clears `ERR` and `PERSISTENT_FAILURE`. While the mismatch persists `ERR` latches again whatever a write does, so the writes of 0 and of 1 to `ERR` are logged only (OBS-SM-ERR-W0, OBS-SM-ERR-W1C). After that leg, on VCS only, a drawn 9-bit encoding outside the state set of the main state machine design package is forced on its state flops for 2 clocks, and `ERR` and `PERSISTENT_FAILURE` after it are logged only (OBS-SM-ILLEGAL, OBS-SM-ILLEGAL-PF): no .adoc or RDL lists the legal encodings of `MAIN_SM_STATUS.STATE`, so no specification makes the forced value an illegal state. Under Verilator the leg logs `INFO-SM-ILLEGAL-SKIP`. Each trip that ends by a MODULE_ENABLE cycle also grades PIC source 16: with `INTR_ENABLE.PERSISTENT_FAILURE` set and every other `INTR_STATUS` bit cleared, vector bit 15 reads 1 (CHK-PIC16; `hw/sys/sep/doc/interrupts.adoc:23`, `:109-111`; `entropy_source.adoc:164`). The entry `sep_esrc_persistent_alert_rand_test_shard2` (`+sep_shard=2`) runs the reset leg and this leg. The entry `sep_esrc_persistent_alert_rand_test_shard3` (`+sep_shard=3`) runs the reset leg and the threshold blocks 0, 5 and 7 on every seed, in a drawn order, with no no-trip leg, and does not grade CHK-PF-NOTRIP and CHK-PF-TRIP-HOLD (it logs `OBS-BLOCKED CHK-PF-TRIP-HOLD` with the reason at each trip).

|<<sep_esrc_autotune_detune_test,`sep_esrc_autotune_detune_test`>>

|no_cpu, `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+esrc_noise_force`

The detune checks grade the detune probe, an observation tap. A sample-clock divide leg disables the generator, clears `SAMPLE_CLK_SELECT` bit 0 and writes each `SAMPLE_CLK_DIVIDE` value 0 to 5 in a drawn order, then one drawn value from 6 to 31; four consecutive lane sample-clock periods equal the ratio (1, 2, 4, 8, 16, 32; divide-by-4 outside 0 to 5) times the source period, exact in ps, and the divide, select, ring-oscillator enable and module enable registers read back the written or reset values (CHK-SCLK-DIV; `hw/ip/entropy_source/doc/architecture.adoc:75`; `hw/ip/entropy_source/regs/entropy_source.rdl:595`, `:674`). CTRL-SCLK-SRC shows that the source clock runs at the bench period. Which enable disables a generator is not stated; the leg clears both and logs the readbacks (INFO-SCLK-DISABLE). A one-enable step clears one enable at a time and logs the lane edges and periods (`OBS-SCLK-ONE-ENABLE`), and `ANY_FAIL_COUNT` is logged around each burst (`OBS-ESRC-ANY-FAIL-GEN`). The tuning bins sample only after their checks pass (INFO-TUNE-BIN-RELEASE logs the release). Shard 1 runs the manual, off and ignored legs, the first burst and the divide leg; shard 2 runs bursts 1, 2 and 3 after the same set-up.

The first 32 churned words XOR the incoming word are logged only (`OBS-FIFO-CHURN-FIRST32`).

With a seeded bit sequence on one lane's noise source, one bit per sample-clock edge, each `NOISE_OBS_RDATA` word holds 32 consecutive samples, LSB first, in order with none lost or repeated, and a value that the sampling flop captures reaches the lane's synchronizer output on the 2nd `clk_i` rising edge after the capture (a two-stage synchronizer). The lost and repeated samples at a sample period near two `clk_i` periods, the level around a `LANE_SEL` write that keeps lane 0 and the words that a full noise FIFO keeps are logged only (`OBS-OBS-LANESEL-SAME`, `OBS-OBS-OVF-KEEP`).

CTRL-EDN-FIFO-HELD shows, on a same-seed trial with no pulse, that the k words land: the FIFO errs at word 14 - k of test data. CTRL-EDN-FIFO-PRECLEAR shows the k words held with `ERR_CODE` 0 before the pulse. CHK-DRBG-ERRCODE grades the `INTR_ENABLE` readback. The coverage bins of a cell or trial sample only after all its checks pass, and the first FAIL closes the coverage window for the rest of the run. The other source bits and the type bits of `ERR_CODE` are logged only (`OBS-DRBG-ERRCODE-FIELDS`).

A write to `RESEED_INTERVAL` and to `INT_STATE_NUM` under the lock is logged only (`OBS-REGWEN-OTHER`): the `csrng.rdl` `REGWEN` text ("all writeable registers ... become read-only") names no register, the upstream `regwen` binding locks only `CTRL`, `FIPS_FORCE` and `ERR_CODE_TEST`, and the two texts differ for these registers, so no golden is stated.

|<<sep_drbg_cmd_sequence_rand_test,`sep_drbg_cmd_sequence_rand_test`>>
|3
|no_cpu, `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+esrc_noise_force`
|CPU-LSU master, `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o`; DRBG seed ingress tap `esrc_compress_vld_o`, `drbg_seed_word_ready_o` (observation tap); generator enable probe `esrc_ro_enable_o` (CHK-CHK1-RO-ENABLE of the entropy bring-up)
|Proves the EDN boot-mode precedence, the software command handshake, that an instantiate sent to an instantiated CSRNG sets the sequence alert, that the EDN boot words reset to 0x901 and 0xFFF003 with clen 0, that the non-zero EDN acknowledge (CMD_STS INVALID_ACMD) of a reserved acmd sets EDN CSRNG_ACK_ERR, that an invalid multi-bit boolean in any CSRNG or EDN CTRL field sets its RECOV_ALERT_STS bit, that a reserved acmd (the drawn 0x0, 0x6, 0x7, 0x8, 0xE or 0xF; 0x9 is logged only in OBS-CSRNG-ACMD-HI, because the CSRNG decodes acmd bits [2:0] only and no text states the result of 0x9 to 0xD) sets bit 13 with CMD_STS INVALID_ACMD and an invalid flag0 sets bit 4, that a Generate past RESEED_INTERVAL is refused with CMD_STS 4 and bit 15 and a Reseed restores the count (the next Generate acks with CMD_STS SUCCESS and four GENBITS words), that GENBITS reads 0 while SW_APP_ENABLE is not True (a known-answer Generate against the CTR_DRBG golden, with flag0 True, returns the golden words: CHK-CSRNG-KAT; `hw/ip/drbg/doc/architecture.adoc:14`), and that in auto request mode the first hardware command after the software instantiate is a Generate with BOOT_MODE 0 (CHK-EDN-AUTO; the first command with MAX_NUM_REQS_BETWEEN_RESEEDS 0 is logged only, OBS-EDN-AUTO-ZERO). With MAX_NUM_REQS_BETWEEN_RESEEDS N of 1 and of a drawn 2 to 4, the command words on the EDN to CSRNG port are the Instantiate, N Generates and one Reseed (CHK-EDN-AUTO-SEQ; `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/edn/regs/gen/adoc/edn.adoc:107`, `:258-262`; CTRL-EDN-AUTO-TAP shows that the tap sees the Instantiate word). With the ESRC generators off, an EDN Instantiate in auto request mode gets no acknowledge for a drawn hold of 256 to 2048 clocks, with no seed handshake and no rise of vector bit 27 in the hold; after the generators start, it gets its acknowledge after a seed handshake with CMD_STS success, and the next command is a Generate (CHK-EDN-INST-SEED; `doc/programmer/src/sep-programming.adoc:457-462`, `:466-468`). Each graded EDN boot (auto true and false) runs twice, once with no coverage window open and once after a pass, and a boot first writes the boot instantiate word 0x601 (flag0 True, clen 0, seed of zero; `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/csrng/doc/theory_of_operation.md:226`). CHK-EDN-INVSEQ reads bit 14 of RECOV_ALERT_STS as 1 before it clears the bit. The leg order is: ..., reseed, auto (first command, N = 1, drawn N, N = 0 logged), instantiate-seed, 0x9, enable order. The draws of N and of the hold follow the draw of the known-answer seed. The `MAIN_SM_STATE` of both units at five steps, the result of enabling EDN, CSRNG and ESRC in that order, and the ESRC words that the DRBG seed adapter accepts and drops are logged only (`OBS-DRBG-MAIN-SM`, `OBS-DRBG-ENABLE-ORDER`, `OBS-DRBG-SEED-INGRESS`).

Each EDN endpoint holds one 8-entry FIFO: with the external select, the held internal endpoint shows 8 entries (pushes minus pops) after 64 held clocks with no push, with at least 8 pushes and no pop (CHK-EDN-FIFO-DEPTH; `hw/sys/sep/doc/trng.adoc:30-31`), and the drain control returns at least 8 words, the first one the held word (CTRL-EDN-FIFO-DRAIN). The largest push gap is logged only (INFO-EDN-PUSH-GAP). The first reads of the select and the lock return their reset values 0x7 and 0x0 (CHK-SEL-RESET; `hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/blocks/sep_cpu_ctrl.adoc:367`, `:379`). A pool pop with a stream selected as external and no source must answer SLVERR on an empty pool and OKAY otherwise (CHK-EXT-NOSRC-POP); the levels and data of that pop, and the beat that a source drops at the end of a leg, are logged only (OBS-EXT-NOSRC, OBS-EXT-WITHDRAW). The bins of the external beat, the stall, the pool pop and the FIFO depth sample only after the check of their leg passes.

==== Fabric and remap

[cols="3,1,3,3,6",options="header"]

|cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip, CPU FW
|Proves the CPU-side address alias window remaps both data and instruction accesses to physical SRAM, that every IFU read carries the AxPROT that `cpu.adoc` states (`sep-cpu-axprot-table`), and that every IFU read on PR-IFU is a single beat, AxLEN 0 (CHK-IFU-SINGLE-BEAT; `fabric.adoc`: the CPU masters issue single-beat transactions only).
|<<sep_fabric_inbound_filter_rule_matrix_test,`sep_fabric_inbound_filter_rule_matrix_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, real PROD fuse sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|Brings the lifecycle controller up in PROD so the inbound filter is genuinely active (`sep_debug=0`, value-checked against the lifecycle golden), then walks entries 0 and 7, two address windows, and `{rw, read-only, write-only}` at `src_id=0` (match-all), plus entry 0 / window 0 × `{rw, r, w}` at `src_id=0x5` with a matching AXI `user[3:0]` and one src-mismatch cell — 16 cells in one invocation — then the same-page `allow_burst=1` granule, page-bound, and `FILTER_CONFIG.locked` on entry 15. `CHK-CONFIG-LOCK` requires both writes to the locked `FILTER_CONFIG` to complete DECERR, and the frozen `allow_burst` to still pass a 2-beat read inside the widened page. A denied burst is graded on a live Lite port (the single-beat access reaches it as one single), and each refused `FILTER_CONFIG` write is graded against a read of the same register made just before it (`CHK-CONFIG-LOCK`). `cp_lite_split` and `cp_locked_write` sample in owner window 757; `cp_lite_split` waits for the `lite_split_pass` label. The R beats of the denied 2-beat read are graded on the `m_axi` pins: two beats, RRESP DECERR, RID equal to a drawn non-zero ARID, RLAST on the last beat only (`CHK-BURST-DENY`). An external read of the Reserved row base 0x10050000 through an allow entry answers DECERR with 0xBADCAB1E (`CHK-FILT-RSVD-READ`); a read of a staged SRAM word through the same entry answers OKAY with that word first (`CTRL-FILT-RSVD-ALLOW`), and the Reserved read shows one AR past the filter at the local crossbar's `ext` initiator (`CTRL-FILT-RSVD-PASSED`). Before the source-ID mismatch cell, the entry reads back as programmed and admits a read with the matching `user[3:0]` (`CTRL-SRC-ID-CFG`). Two logged legs record the response and read data of each denied read (`OBS-FILT-DENY-RDATA`), and whether one external read sent before fuse sensing ends, after an `rst_ni` pulse at the end of the leaf, is accepted and answered (`OBS-SI-PRESENSE-READ`); neither is graded.

[[phase2-mailbox-irq-pic]]
==== Mailbox, IRQ, PIC

[cols="3,1,3,3,6",options="header"]

|Test Case |Phase |Run mode |VIP / Model |Description
|<<sep_mailbox_plic_test,`sep_mailbox_plic_test`>>
|1
|cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip, CPU FW
|Completes the inbound-aperture interrupt path all the way to the CPU. Also proves that a 32-bit store to the low or the high half of the outbound `WRITE_DATA` of a drawn mailbox pushes the other half as 0: the 64-bit pop returns the word in the written half and 0 in the other half (CHK-HALF-ZERO), with the bench lane inject putting non-zero data on the unstrobed lanes and the mailbox unit tap showing that beat (CHK-HALF-ZERO-LANES). The pop response is logged (`OBS-HALF-ZERO-RESP`).
|<<sep_irq_ip_to_aggregator_test,`sep_irq_ip_to_aggregator_test`>>
|1
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|Proves that interrupts owned by the entropy blocks, HMAC error, and DMA done/chunk/error reach the SEP interrupt aggregator on the correct bit, using each block's real interrupt-test register rather than a forced wire. Also latches the DMA register-path bus-error bit from both a read and a write, and the HMAC, KMAC and OTBN peripheral-bridge bits with a through-adapter SLVERR, watches aggregator bits 40 and 42, and clears each.
|<<sep_periph_bus_err_misaligned_test,`sep_periph_bus_err_misaligned_test`>>
|1
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip

The aligned read and the narrow read at the same offset complete with OKAY and latch nothing. The probe runs twice: after the first pass both latches are cleared (`CHK-MISALIGN-CLEAR`) and the second pass runs in owner window 763 (`cp_partial_narrow`).
|<<sep_axil_mailbox_iface_rand_test,`sep_axil_mailbox_iface_rand_test`>>

Also walks the inbound filter allow and deny over that live peer path, each the other's control.

Every peer access carries seeded inbound request attributes and is graded on its response code and response ID.

Logs the response of a read of each write-only register and of a write to each read-only register (`OBS-SPI-WO-RO-ACCESS`) and READY, TXEMPTY, RXEMPTY and BYTEORDER at reset (`OBS-SPI-STATUS-RESET`).

The write response of each refused write is logged (`OBS-LOCK-WR-RESP`) and not graded. Every access runs twice, first with the coverage window closed, then in owner window 754, where each FCOV sample waits for the checks of its access; at each rise of `[33]` no other vector bit moves (`CHK-SRC34-EXCL`).

Also proves that the error cause drives its line only when `IRQEN.eirq` is set, and that a 32-bit read of the upper half of `ERROR_FLAGS` returns 0 and clears both flags. An empty 64-bit `READ_DATA` read returns 0x0000_0000_FEED_DEAD, both halves graded (`CHK-ERR-RD`). The high 32 bits of a `WRITE_DATA` read are logged (`OBS-MBX-DATA-HI`) with no verdict.

|<<sep_pic_reg_rand_test,`sep_pic_reg_rand_test`>>

|cpu, +skip_fuse_sense

The hole, entry-0, `meigwclr` and source-0 writes, which those checks grade by an unchanged or 0 read, are counted on the same strobe (CHK-PIC-STORE). The reset X check samples `pic_rd_data_probe_o`, an observation tap the test adds to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list.

|3
