|ocah_axi_vip, CPU FW
|Proves that an external read or write to each sampled PIC address answers DECERR and changes no PIC register, in both the default-block and the open-window filter regime. The sampled addresses are the first, a drawn interior and the last register of each PIC group, a drawn hole and a drawn past-extent address; the drawn addresses change with the seed. The default-block control holds with the sensed LC state PROD and `SEP_DBG` off, read from the DUT.
|<<sep_pic_priority_gate_rand_test,`sep_pic_priority_gate_rand_test`>>
|3
|cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|CPU FW, PIC priority golden, `timer_int_i` (added hook, top-port drive)
|Proves that a pending source runs only when enabled, above the never priority and with `mstatus.MIE` set. Also proves that the highest effective priority runs first in either priority order, that a tie at the top runs the lowest source ID first and every later delivery of a round follows the same order (CHK-WINNER, CHK-FLIP), that a source at the `meipt` threshold stays pending and one level above it is delivered (CHK-THRESHOLD), that with `meicurpl` set to the level of the running handler a source at that level waits and one level above nests (CHK-NEST), that each internal timer traps with its own cause only with its `mie.MITIE` bit set and not through `mie.MTIE` or `mie.MSIE` (CHK-INT-TIMER-HOLD, CHK-INT-TIMER-TRAP), and that no timer or software trap is taken. A source pending at an open PIC gate with `mstatus.MIE` set does not deliver while `mie.MEIE` is 0 and delivers once with `mie.MEIE` set (CHK-GATED). A pending source with `meie` 0 reads 1 in `meip` (CHK-GATED, `closed_meip`). `timer_int_i` driven to 1 sets `mip.MTIP`, takes no trap with `mie.MTIE` clear and one trap with `mcause` 0x8000_0007 with `mie.MTIE` set (CHK-TIMER-PIN). Each of the 16 `meipl` encodings reads back as written in both priority orders, with `mpiccfg.priord` read back at the order of the cell (CHK-LEVEL-SWEEP).
|<<sep_pic_pending_map_walk_rand_test,`sep_pic_pending_map_walk_rand_test`>>
|3
|cpu, +skip_fuse_sense, +esrc_noise_force
|CPU FW, PIC pending-map golden
|Proves that each of the 24 driven map sources (1 to 8, 12, 14, 18 to 30 and 33) sets only its own pending bit in `meip`, that the 11 crypto alert channels raise source 33, and that level and edge gateways follow the `meigwctrl` and `meigwclr` text. Tied-off and slotless cases move nothing. Also proves that one asserted boundary input bit shows as one pending source above 43 and drops on release. The alert index leg samples `crypto_alert_pulse_probe_o`, an observation tap the test adds to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list. The boundary input leg drives `sep_ext_interrupts_i`, a top-port drive the test adds to `docs/SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`. The line shape of each map source (`OBS-SRC-SHAPE`) is logged only.

|3
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense

|Proves that a faulting CPU access latches only its own block bit, that source 43 is the OR of the block bits, and that each clear bit clears only its own block. Also proves that a non-OKAY response to a DMA source read latches `host_path_err` alone and raises source 42, that `DMA_BUS_ERR_CLEAR` clears both DMA latches only on a written 1, that a write of 1 to `STATUS.ERROR` leaves `host_path_err` and source 42 set, and that a fault in the cycle of its own clear still latches. The same-cycle legs sample `sep_cpu_ctrl_err_pulse_probe_o`, an observation tap the test adds to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list. The DMA host-path legs use the response control of the outbound responder, an error inject the test adds to `docs/SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`. Also proves that a partial write to AES `CTRL_AUX_REGWEN` and to the watchdog `WDOG_BARK_THOLD` answers SLVERR and latches only that block bit and source 43 (`CHK-PARTIAL-WR`, `CHK-PARTIAL-CLR`; the register value after the refused write is logged as `OBS-PARTIAL-KEPT`, because no specification sentence states it), and that no other vector bit moves at the rises of sources 41, 42 and 43 (`CHK-SRC41-EXCL`, `CHK-SRC42-EXCL`, `CHK-SRC43-EXCL`). The owned bins sample on the `bfault_pass` label, except `cp_src_exclusive_other[s41, s42, s43]`, which sample on the `exo_pass` label after the EXCL check of their source passed at the end of the run. With the graded window closed, right after the latch step, the test reads one unbacked word of each of six other register blocks from the CPU-LSU master and one HMAC hole word from the external master, and logs the response, the read data and the latch state of each (`OBS-BRIDGE-HOLE`), with no verdict on the latch state. The external read of the HMAC hole answers SLVERR with 0xFFFFFFFF, the Hole in Extent cell of the HMAC row (`CHK-BRIDGE-HOLE-SI`).

==== SPI and DMA

|cpu, +skip_fuse_sense

|Drives the secure DMA engine from real firmware to copy a block from SRAM into the CPU's data tightly-coupled memory with the inline hash engine enabled, then proves two things independently: the copied data is identical to the source, and the hardware digest is correct. Walks both SHA-256, against a software digest over build-varying data, and SHA-384, against the published FIPS 180-4 vector for a fixed message. Also carries the same message as two chunks and re-runs it with the digest byte-swap off.

|cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip, ocah_spi_vip, CPU FW

Also proves that `ERROR_STATUS.OVERFLOW` is clear at TXFULL and set by the next write.
|<<sep_spi_ot_flash_cmd_rand_test,`sep_spi_ot_flash_cmd_rand_test`>>

|cpu, +skip_fuse_sense

A lane leg runs the dual and quad reads 0x3B, 0x6B, 0xBB and 0xEB and the quad page program 0x32, graded on the lanes, in the flash record and in firmware. The primary page program and its READ are graded the same way at x1.
|<<sep_dma_basic_test,`sep_dma_basic_test`>>
|2
|cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip, CPU FW
|Secure-DMA control and copy breadth over the CPU LSU: documented reset values, the configuration lock reading locked while the engine is observed busy, the range-valid gate and its rw0c lock each with a positive control, FIXED/INCR/WRAP against a per-mode golden, 1/2/4-byte widths, `STATUS.done` RW1C, one invalid-opcode error with recovery, the address, address-space-id and transfer-width refusals, the inverted-range refusal, a copy that leaves SRAM for the instruction memory and back, a DMA-issued host-path command-integrity inject (`host_path_err` / aggregator bit 41), and a fabric DECERR dest that raises the same bit with the pin low. The mode and width walk is directed. Every ERROR_CODE compare grades the named fields alone (CHK-RANGE-REGWEN grades `range_valid_error` alone), the reset values are graded on the named fields with the reserved bits logged (`OBS-RESET-RSVD`, `OBS-ERR-RSVD`), and the host requires the PASS line of each error-leg check (CHK-ERR-CLASS).
|<<sep_spi_pad_speed_dir_matrix_test,`sep_spi_pad_speed_dir_matrix_test`>>
|3
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip, ocah_spi_vip, SPI pad ports, lane and output-enable taps (approved)
|Proves that every legal speed and direction cell gives the stated number of sck cycles, lane data at x1, x2 and x4 in both directions and the stated data lane output enables per segment on the lanes it uses, x2 and x4 dummy segments included (an x1 RX-only segment and an x1 dummy segment are logged only; lanes 3:2 at x1 and x2 are logged and are graded at 0b00 only through the SPI data scoreboard, which decodes a TX segment only when its four output enables equal the segment's lanes), that chained segments hold chip select low and carry each segment's data at its own width, and that the five illegal cells other than SPEED 3 with DIRECTION 2 raise CMDINVAL. It also proves that the last RX word of a segment whose byte count is not a multiple of 4 has its unused most significant bytes at 0 (CHK-SPI-RX-PAD).
|<<sep_spi_pad_timing_cfg_rand_test,`sep_spi_pad_timing_cfg_rand_test`>>
|3
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip, ocah_spi_vip, SPI pad ports, lane and output-enable taps (approved)
|Proves that CPOL, CPHA, CLKDIV, CSNLEAD, CSNTRAIL and CSNIDLE set the sck idle level, period, chip-select spacing and Tx data edge at the pad, in two drawn CPOL, CPHA and FULLCYC cells per seed, one at each CPOL and each CPHA value. Each cell runs at a drawn lane width, and its Tx and Rx data are graded byte for byte on the lanes. Each cell has exactly the frames it registered, and each Rx segment delivers exactly its RXDATA words. The Tx data edge is not graded in the cell CPHA=1 with FULLCYC=1, because two OpenTitan texts give that cell different shift edges (`OBS-SPI-CPHA`); the CPHA=1 edge closes on a merge of regressions. At CPHA=0 and at CPHA=1, at x1, x2 and x4 on every seed, an Rx segment from a late responder reads the seeded bytes with FULLCYC=1 and one beat late with FULLCYC=0 (control), so the FULLCYC sampling point is graded. With CONTROL.OUTPUT_EN=1 the chip-select and clock output enables are on in every frame, in the gaps and while chip select is high, and both are off before the first OUTPUT_EN write (control). CSNLEAD, CSNTRAIL and CSNIDLE are graded as the minimum bounds the OpenTitan theory of operation states, and their margin is logged.
|<<sep_spi_fifo_stall_watermark_rand_test,`sep_spi_fifo_stall_watermark_rand_test`>>
|3
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip, ocah_spi_vip, SPI pad ports, lane and output-enable taps (approved)
|Proves, at x1, x2 and x4 on every seed, that TXSTALL and RXSTALL pause sck, that the watermark flags follow the occupancy at watermark 0, above the depth, at a drawn TX control watermark and on the RX axis as RXQD rises and falls, and that the full flags, the idle TXQD and RXQD, the RXQD step per read and the command queue follow the words moved. It also proves that the TX FIFO takes 73 words before TXFULL, that an idle 64-word Rx segment gives RXFULL and RXQD 64, and that CMDQD stays at 4 or below while the leaf fills the command queue behind a held segment (`spi_host.hjson:93-107`, `spi_host.hjson:318-319`, `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/spi_host/doc/theory_of_operation.md:684-685`). Also proves at each width the Tx and Rx data of every frame, INTR_STATE.SPI_EVENT for the watermark events, and no data lane driven and the chip-select and clock output enables on after a SW_RST abort. The RX words held past RXFULL are logged only: no source states them. The CMDQD at which READY stays 0 and the head-segment count of CMDQD are logged only: the OpenTitan texts give no single full point. The CMDQD change after each accepted COMMAND is logged only: the OpenTitan texts on CMDQD and on the command clock domain crossing conflict. The TXSTALL, ACTIVE and chip-select level of the STATUS read before the first TXDATA word of a Tx segment, and the fields of the read at the first TXSTALL, are logged only (`OBS-SPI-TXSTALL`).
|<<sep_spi_ot_host_csr_irq_rand_test_leg,`sep_spi_ot_host_csr_irq_rand_test`>> (extension)

|2
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|Wakeup and watchdog tick at the stated rates (one count per tick, one wakeup count per prescaler + 1 ticks, graded as the exact count difference of two stopped runs), threshold expiry at the stated point (no expiry for a stopped run that ends below the threshold, expiry for one that ends above it, and a stopped count at or above the threshold after the polled set), wakeup cause and its RW1C (the expiry flag reads set right before its RW1C), the watchdog pet, and the REGWEN lock on the AON timer (`regwen` reads 1 before the lock write; both thresholds keep their whole pre-lock values under a locked write, after pre-lock writes of both landed), plus the `clk_ref_i` reference counter advancing. The software LOAD of that counter is NOT claimed -- a fast `clk_i` against `clk_ref_i` can drop it; the detail entry states why. Bark and bite are not claimed here: `sep_nmi_sanity_test` and `sep_reset_wdt_sanity_test` own those legs, and no checker in this entry observes either. The wakeup count at the edge where the expiry sets is logged from the wakeup tap (`OBS-WKUP-EXPIRE-COUNT`), with no verdict.
|<<sep_clock_gate_control_test,`sep_clock_gate_control_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|Proves that the one implemented `CLOCK_GATE_CTRL` bit (`pka_cg_enable`) stores in both directions, also under an all-ones write to both halves, and that a change of the bit leaves four witness registers unchanged: AES `STATUS`, HMAC `INTR_STATE`, OTBN `STATUS` and `SW_DEBUG` read the same value with it set and clear. These witnesses are constant in this build, so the compare catches an enable that disturbs them and does not by itself prove that the bit gates no clock. `CHK-STORAGE` reads both 32-bit halves without a mask and grades the `pka_cg_enable` field for the write of 1 and the write of 0. `CHK-CELL-STORAGE` grades the same field in each witness cell and is the positive probe of `CHK-STUB-CONST`. The Reserved bits 63:1 have no stated reset or read value; they are logged as `OBS-CG-RSVD` (and `OBS-CG-RSVD-ALLONES` after the all-ones write) and not graded. Every check runs with the owner window closed. After all pass, two graded `CHK-STORAGE` steps (write 0, write 1) run, each in its own owner window 772; `sep_cpu_ctrl_reg_cg.cp_clock_gate_ctrl` samples only there.
|<<sep_boot_release_terms_test,`sep_boot_release_terms_test`>>
|3
|no_cpu, real fuse sense; the second testlist entry `sep_boot_release_terms_test_smc_hold` adds the bench plusarg `+sep_smc_fuse_sense_hold`
|JTAG AXI-Lite master (before the release), LSU read path (after it), reset taps
|Proves that both SEP resets release only after `rst_ni`, fuse sensing and `ext_boot_seq_done_i` are all high, in a held case in every leaf and in four arrival orders that the seed draws (`CTRL-REL-ORDER` requires the sampled order of the three terms to match the drawn order), and that `SMC_FUSE_SENSE_STATUS` follows the SMC input. Proves that a shadow-map read before sensing completes answers SLVERR. The SEP core is held in reset until sensing completes, so every read before the release runs on the OTP JTAG port; `CTRL-PRESENSE-PATH` requires a JTAG read of `EFUSE_INTERFACE_CTRL_STATUS` (outside the token block) in the same window to answer OKAY, so the SLVERR is the shadow-map refusal. Every read after the release runs on the core load path. `CHK-SENSE-STATUS` grades that the reads of `SEP_FUSE_SENSE_STATUS` after a settle wait of 64 `clk_i` cycles from the port rise complete with the port at 1 (the response code is logged). The register description (`hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/blocks/sep_cpu_ctrl.adoc`) states no value of the field `sep_fuse_sense_done` or of the reserved bits 63:1 after sensing, so the read words are logged as `OBS-SENSE-STATUS-DONE` (an open specification question) and not graded. The register block is reset by `sep_reset_n`, and no specification line states that it answers a read while sensing runs, so the leaf issues no read of it before the release and logs `INFO-SENSE-STATUS-PRE`. Owner window 513 holds the pass label `rel_terms_pass`: `cp_release_order[release_after_sense_rise]` samples on the rise of bit 0, after `CHK-REL-GATE` of the epoch passes. A FAIL closes the window and drops a pending sample. The bin `release_after_sense_rise` depends on the seed: it fills only when the epoch with the SMC input high releases while the sense-done rise is still ahead. Extends the open SEC_DIS and eFuse sense tests with the boot-term, SMC and pre-sense cells only. In the hold leaf the test also sends one LSU read to the SMC aperture while the SMC input is 0 and logs the SMC input level, the address and the AR count at the SMC tap (`OBS-SMC-PRESENSE-RESP`), with no verdict; the response comes from the bench SMC responder and is not logged.

One cold reset with `mpc_reset_run_req` 0 runs after every check: no instruction retires in W_obs after the reset release (CHK-RUNREQ0-HALT; EL2 PRM, Multi-Core Debug Control and Status Signals: 0 starts the core in Debug Mode halted), and the instruction fetch requests are logged (OBS-RUNREQ0-FETCH). |<<sep_efuse_image_test,`sep_efuse_image_test`>>
|1
|no_cpu, real fuse sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|Proves the write-one-to-set programming path into one-time-programmable memory: bits that are burned read back as one directly from the array before any re-sense, and after a cold re-sense the shadow equals the original image plus exactly the bits that were burned. `CHK-PRE-SENSE-FAIL-CLOSED` requires the whole 64-bit `FEAT_CTRL` to read 0 before sense done. `CHK-SENSE-SHADOW` requires every shadow word to equal the image, the judged count to equal the reads issued, and `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` to be 1 after the read; the `sep_fuse_sense_done` field of `SEP_FUSE_SENSE_STATUS` and the reserved status bits have no stated value and are logged as `OBS-SENSE-STATUS-DONE` and `OBS-SENSE-STATUS-RSVD`. `CHK-W1S-NOCLOBBER` requires the whole direct OTP word to equal the image word with bits 0 and 1 at 0 before their two programs, and the image word plus bits 0 and 1 after them; the random burn never draws those two bits. Owner window 768 runs from the release to the end of `CHK-SENSE-SHADOW`. The bins `cp_presense_feat[invalid_profile_zero]` and `cp_sense_surface[status1_port1]` hold a legal outcome as pending and sample on the rise of the pass label `efimg_pass`, which the leaf writes after the owning check passes; an illegal outcome samples at once.
|<<sep_efuse_km_axil_cpu_mux_coexist_test,`sep_efuse_km_axil_cpu_mux_coexist_test`>>
|1
|cpu, real fuse sense
|ocah_axi_vip, CPU FW
|Proves that the eFuse AXI-Lite mux serves the CPU and the Key Manager at the same time: the chiplet identifier reads its known value on every CPU iteration, the Key Manager's ownership tags stay intact, and its register payload keeps advancing.
|<<sep_efuse_lcc_lc_state_stitch_test,`sep_efuse_lcc_lc_state_stitch_test`>>
|1
|no_cpu, real fuse sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|Walks the device through four lifecycle states — one preloaded, three programmed for real into one-time-programmable memory — and proves each reaches the lifecycle controller and produces the expected feature-control decode. At every state of the walk `CHK-SECURE-TM-OFF` or `CHK-SECURE-TM-ON` requires `secure_tm_o` to equal the `TEST_EN` strap driven before that sense, with SEC_DIS at 0. Owner window 771 runs from before the first sense to the last chain check. The bins `cp_stm_event_x_strap[sense_done_strap0, sense_done_strap1]` and `cp_transition[test_dev_to_prod_prog]` hold the event as pending and sample on the rise of the pass label `stitch_state_pass`, which the leaf writes after every check of that state passes. Nothing is graded after the integrity-error inject falls; `OBS-LC-SIGINT-RELEASE` logs `sep_ras_o.lc_sigint_err`, `FEAT_CTRL` and `lcc_feat_ctrl_probe_o` two clocks after it.
|<<sep_efuse_jtag_axil_el2_cpu_mux_test,`sep_efuse_jtag_axil_el2_cpu_mux_test`>>
|1
|cpu, real fuse sense
|ocah_axi_vip, CPU FW
|The debug-port counterpart to the two-processor coexistence test. A real-sensed PROD image makes the JTAG path LC-restricted, so one image proves allowed-MMR coexistence and the LC-gated shadow deny while the CPU reads the same eFuse port.

|no_cpu, real fuse sense

|1
|no_cpu, real fuse sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|Proves that the lifecycle security state actually gates the external inbound port — the security-boundary property, not just the register that describes it. With the filter bypassed, the port still reaches Scratch SRAM and still gets DECERR at the Boot ROM, Reset Ctrl, ICCM, DCCM, PIC and AP/STEE remap regions, which the connectivity matrix gives it no path to.

|2
|no_cpu, real fuse sense

|`EFUSE_PROGRAM_CTRL.efuse_program_read_back` selects the verified program: against a dropped bank write the read-back program reports `program_status` 1 and the plain program reports 0; both program the bit when the write is kept.

|2
|no_cpu, real fuse sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|Proves that every demote cell produces the `FEAT_CTRL` golden and the `dbg_disable` ladder and gates live external access, and that the demote lock blocks a later demote until `rst_ni`. In each cell `CHK-FEAT-CTRL`, `CHK-LIVE-GATE`, `CHK-DBG-DISABLE` (the 11-bit claimed vector as one word) and `CHK-FUSE-DFT-DIS` (class-relative: each fuse DFT port equals the control of its open or closed class, and the two controls differ) run once in owner window 769. `CTRL-DEMOTE-CLEAR` requires `DEMOTE_1` and `DEMOTE_2` to read `demote` 0 and `lock` 0 before each undemoted cell, so a cell whose golden ignores the demotion still runs undemoted. `CHK-DEMOTE-LOCK` sets one `rsvd` marker bit in the write that the lock ignores and reads it back, so a dropped write fails; `OBS-LCC-WR-RESP` logs that write's response and `OBS-DTP-PORT-LEVEL` logs the two levels of each fuse DFT port. `x_path_case` and `x_prod_demote_case3` hold the legal cells of the cell's `FEAT_CTRL` read as pending and sample on the rise of the pass label `dem_pass`, which the leaf writes after every check of that cell passes; a FAIL closes the window and drops them.

The SEP core is held in reset until sensing completes, so while sense is stopped the OTP JTAG port presents the tokens and makes the retained-window eFuse accesses, and each frontdoor read runs inside an override release. `CHK-SENSE-GATED-RESET` requires that the JTAG match leaves the reset held. `CHK-FEAT-PRESENSE` requires that, while the match holds the reset and sensing is open, `lcc_feat_ctrl_probe_o` reads all ones on the clock where both reset probes read 0, with an override release as the control. `CHK-LC-STATE-PORT` requires `lc_state_o` to read 0x0F on the first `clk_i` edge after the first `rst_ni` release. `CHK-DBG-DISABLE` requires each `dbg_disable_<path>_o` to equal its case golden before the token and after the stopped-sense match. `CHK-FUSE-DFT-CLASS` requires each fuse DFT port to equal its closed control before the token and its open control under the match, with the two controls differing; `OBS-DTP-PORT-LEVEL` logs the levels. `CHK-OVR-AFTER` requires the same override inputs to release and re-hold the reset after the match. `CHK-MAP-OPEN` requires an OKAY `LC_STATE` shadow-map read in that window. `CHK-SENSE-STUCK` requires that the match stops the sense. After a `rst_ni` pulse with the token kept, the `CHK-RETAIN-*` checkers require SEC_DIS to stay active, sense to stay stopped, the map read to return OKAY and the override to release the reset. `CHK-DROP` then clears SEC_DIS, and `CHK-SENSE-CONTROL` requires a full sense after a second pulse to finish inside the stuck window. In the retained SEC_DIS window (sense stopped, every shadow at its reset value), `OBS-SECDIS-SHADOW-WR` logs the response and the JTAG readback of a JTAG write of a 0 bit of `REQUIRED_SIGNERS`, without a verdict, because no line states a shadow write before sense under SEC_DIS (`hw/sys/sep/doc/otp_fuse_controller.adoc:66`, `:121`, `:325` and `hw/sys/sep/regs/blocks/sep_efuse_map/sep_efuse_map.rdl:846-851` state it after sense); `CTRL-SECDIS-SHADOW-WR` requires the reset value before the write. `CTRL-SECDIS-JTAG-REACH` requires each JTAG read of that window to answer AXI OKAY (`hw/sys/sep/doc/otp_fuse_controller.adoc`, Shadow Register and JTAG Access Control: the held INVALID state does not restrict JTAG), so a refused or dead port fails. `CHK-SECDIS-LC-WR` grades that a JTAG write of the PROD encoding to the INVALID `LC_STATE` word leaves its JTAG readback at its reset word `0x0000000f` (`hw/sys/sep/regs/blocks/sep_efuse_map/sep_efuse_map.rdl:573-577`; `hw/sys/sep/doc/lifecycle_controller.adoc:87`, `:90`; `hw/sys/sep/doc/otp_fuse_controller.adoc:220-224`: INVALID is terminal), and `OBS-SECDIS-LC-WR` logs the write response without a verdict. `CHK-RETAIN-LC-RESET` grades that the `rst_ni` pulse under SEC_DIS returns the `LC_STATE` word from its sensed value to its reset value `0x0F` (`hw/sys/sep/doc/security_disable.adoc`, Reset Persistence). `OBS-SECDIS-DBG-BUS` logs the bits of `ext_debug_bus_o` that change while those two writes run and in a quiet window before and after them, without a verdict: no specification puts eFuse state on the bus. The JTAG write response is logged and not graded, because the specification states no response for a write that the terminal lifecycle rule refuses. After the later full sense, `CHK-OVERRIDE-TM0` requires that a fresh match with the `TEST_EN` strap at 0 gives an all-ones `FEAT_CTRL` that equals `lcc_feat_ctrl_probe_o`, with `secure_tm_o` at 0 (`hw/sys/sep/doc/lifecycle_controller.adoc:336`, `:341`; `hw/sys/sep/doc/security_disable.adoc:25`; `hw/sys/sep/doc/test_mode.adoc:12`, `:34`). Three owner windows (770): the first runs from before the first `rst_ni` release to the stopped-sense gate read (`cp_presense_port` and the before-sense and SEC_DIS cells of `x_cond_gate`); the others cover `CHK-OVR-AFTER` (`cp_alone[override_open]`) and the strap-0 match through `CHK-OVERRIDE-TM0` (`x_secdis_stm_feat[secure_tm_0]`). Each event is held as pending and samples on the rise of its bit of the pass label `ovr_pass`, which the leaf writes after that check passes. A FAIL closes the window and drops the pending event.

The positive probes are in the same line: the post-sense JTAG read answers OKAY with the staged word, the sensed-shadow probe equals the staged word, and `CTRL-IF-PRESENSE-JTAG-TWIN` shows that a post-sense JTAG program lands exactly its bit with one program command. The response of the program write is logged as `OBS-IF-PRESENSE-JTAG-RESP`. The case runs a graded leg with the owner window closed and then a window leg in owner window 625, which runs only after the graded leg passes; `sep_efuse_jtag_policy_cg.x_jtag_presense` samples in the window leg, after its own CHK-IF-PRESENSE-JTAG line passed. |Frontdoor master, `security_disable_o`, `sep_fuse_sense_done_o`, sensed-shadow probe, IC_RESET TDR driver, bound SEC_DIS digest tap and `efuse_shim_command_req_o` tap (observation taps)
|Proves that a SEC_DIS match made before sensing keeps sense done low and the sensed shadow words at their reset values, that a match made during sensing keeps sense done low and the sensed shadow words unchanged, and that every shadow word reads its reset value after a cold reset under the match, with the sense stop, the open map read and the recovery as controls. `CHK-LC-STATE-PORT` requires `lc_state_o` to read 0x0F at those reads in both classes. Class C logs `OBS-SDIS-FIRST-CYCLE`, the feature-control probe on the first `clk_i` edge after the release and on the first edge with `security_disable_o` at 1, because no text states its value there.

|<<sep_efuse_shim_response_fault_test,`sep_efuse_shim_response_fault_test`>>
|3
|no_cpu, real fuse sense, injected shim response
|Sense status inject and shim response hold (added hooks), shim response taps and `efuse_shim_command_req_o` tap (observation taps), read and program interface outputs
|Proves that a sense response with its status bit set leaves its shadow word at the reset value and still lets sense done rise, and that a program request shows busy and not done while its response is withheld and done with no error after it. `CHK-SENSE-STATUS` also grades the sense shape in the control sense and in each injected sense: exactly one READ command at `efuse_shim_cmd_*_o` from the `rst_ni` release to the sense-done rise, one response strobe per word (256), and at the sensing FSM ports (`efuse_shadow_resp_valid_o`, `efuse_shadow_sense_done_o`) the `fuse_sense_done_o` rise exactly two cycles after the cycle of the last strobe (`hw/sys/sep/doc/otp_fuse_controller.adoc`, sensing timing diagram). A dead command tap fails as `read_cmds=0`. The control sense and the first injected sense run with the owner window closed. A second identical injected sense runs in owner window 630 only if every check so far passed, and `cp_sense_shape` samples there. The first FAIL closes the window. A last sense sets the status bit on a drawn control word (shadow words 0 to 9 and 255 whose staged value differs from reset). For words 0 to 9, `CHK-SENSE-STATUS-CTRLWORD` grades that the word reads its RDL reset value and sense done rises. For every drawn word, `CHK-SENSE-STATUS-CTRLWORD-FEAT` grades `lcc_feat_ctrl_probe_o` against the feature-control profile row of the sensed shadows. Word 255 is logged as `OBS-SENSE-STATUS-CTRLWORD` and not graded.
|===

Also relevant, owned by <<phase2-mailbox-irq-pic,Mailbox, IRQ, PIC>>: <<sep_locked_field_access_irq_path_test>> and <<sep_efuse_token_match_fault_pic_test>> reach eFuse state, but their contract is the IRQ/PIC delivery path. They are graded there so each test has one owning bucket.

[[sep-vplan-summary-7]]
==== Key Manager vault

[cols="3,1,3,3,6",options="header"]
|===
|Test Case |Phase |Run mode |VIP / Model |Description
|<<sep_km_otbn_sideload_kat_test,`sep_km_otbn_sideload_kat_test`>>
|1
|no_cpu, real fuse sense
|ocah_axi_vip, KM path / golden
|Proves the full key-distribution chain from the real entropy datapath to a real cryptographic consumer: the OpenTitan entropy stack seeds the Key Manager, the Key Manager's firmware accepts a known 384-bit key over its mailbox and sideloads it to OTBN over the dedicated key bus, and an OTBN program running on the real core reconstructs the key from its two shares and writes it to data memory, where the host reads it back and compares against the known value.
|<<sep_km_aes_sideload_kat_test,`sep_km_aes_sideload_kat_test`>>
|1
|no_cpu, real fuse sense
|ocah_axi_vip, KM path / golden
|Proves that a known key loaded over the Key Manager mailbox and sideloaded to AES encrypts to the independent golden and decrypts back to the plaintext, and that the software-key path reaches the same golden.
|<<sep_km_hmac_sideload_kat_test,`sep_km_hmac_sideload_kat_test`>>
|1
|no_cpu, real fuse sense
|ocah_axi_vip, KM path / golden
|Proves that a known key loaded over the Key Manager mailbox and sideloaded to HMAC produces the independent HMAC-SHA256 golden, and that the digest differs from the golden of the decoy software key.
|<<sep_km_kmac_sideload_kat_test,`sep_km_kmac_sideload_kat_test`>>
|1
|no_cpu, real fuse sense
|ocah_axi_vip, KM path / golden
|Proves that a known key loaded over the Key Manager mailbox and sideloaded to KMAC produces the software-key digest, that this digest equals the independent KMAC256 golden, and that it differs from the decoy-key digest.
|<<sep_km_isolate_termination_test,`sep_km_isolate_termination_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip, KM CPU image
|What a Key Manager MASTER transaction receives when its own AXI-Lite path is isolated. Every other isolation entry drives the SEP host port and so reaches the `host_*` paths only; the KM masters the `km_*` paths, so a KM-CPU image is the vehicle. Five destinations run in a seeded order: AES, KMAC, OTBN, HMAC and ABR. A store to the key aperture of each is OKAY while its path is live (CHK-KM-PATH-LIVE-<DEST>), is SLVERR while the path is isolated, with exactly one B response on that path, `AXI_SLVERR` set and `AXI_DECERR` clear (CHK-KM-ISO-TERM-<DEST>, run twice per destination; `hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc:139-141`), and is OKAY again after release (CHK-KM-ISO-REOPEN-<DEST>; `:144`). A last leg writes the KM `SW_RESET_N` bit: the KM gated reset falls only when the five key paths and the eFuse path all report isolated (CHK-KM-RST-ORDER; `:143`, `:154`).
|<<sep_km_ic_reset_release_test,`sep_km_ic_reset_release_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense, VCS only
|ocah_axi_vip, IC_RESET TDR, KM CPU image
|Stage, apply and release of the IC_RESET `km_jtag_rst_n` override while the KM fetches from its ROM. The override holds the KM and the release restarts it; `prim_rom` `noXOnCsI` grades the ROM request on every cycle of the window after the release.
|<<sep_km_key_policy_vault_test,`sep_km_key_policy_vault_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip, KM path / golden
|The key/policy vault refuses what it should: a store to a locked SRAM region is dropped with the readback unchanged, the matching `SRAM_WRITE_LOCK_VIOLATION` bit sets, a store past the KPV register map raises `IRQ_STATUS` AXI_SLVERR only, and the status bit clears W1C. The seal walk is here too: the same erase must retire a sealed slot and free an unsealed one.
|<<sep_km_command_set_rand_test,`sep_km_command_set_rand_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, real fuse sense
|ocah_axi_vip, KM path / golden
|Produce, move and destroy a key over the KM mailbox; reject illegal, over-long and mis-framed commands, with the received payload length and the computed payload CRC-32C as the argument of the length and payload-CRC rejections, and with the index of the invalid word as the argument of a CMD_KEY_GENERATE with an over-range REQ_SIZE or a reserved DEST_VALID bit. AES is the consume witness. Version queries, OTP cold lock, recoverable-fault ACK, and a directed ABR ML-DSA seed transfer every seed. RANDCFG extra transfer dest (HMAC / KMAC / AES / OTBN) writes one classic `cp_dest` cell per seed. The four sideload KATs are the MUST-fill owners for HMAC/KMAC/AES/OTBN consume.
|<<sep_km_wipe_reset_test,`sep_km_wipe_reset_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, real fuse sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|KM `SW_RESET_N` park/release returns `RESP_KM_READY` and a later `CMD_KEY_LOAD` succeeds. Then `KM_WIPE_CTRL.wipe_state` posts `RESP_UNRECOVERABLE_FAULT`.
|<<sep_km_handover_test,`sep_km_handover_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, real fuse sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|Handover mailbox contract: empty/reserved `CMD_SRAM_*` refusals, `CMD_EXEC_ROM` inhibit, then a 14-word `CMD_SRAM_LOAD_EXEC` stream and a warm-reset `CMD_SRAM_EXEC`.
|<<sep_km_abr_seed_sideload_test,`sep_km_abr_seed_sideload_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, real fuse sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|KM `CMD_KEY_TRANSFER` dest ABR ML-DSA seed, KV pull, KEYGEN public key equals a direct-seed KEYGEN of the same seed words in the same order, and differs from the first seed's public key.
|<<sep_km_abr_mlkem_sideload_test,`sep_km_abr_mlkem_sideload_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, real fuse sense
|ocah_axi_vip, `abr_mlkem_seed_z_probe_o`
|The three ML-KEM lanes of the KV facade: the 16-dword seed lane carrying D then Z, the message lane, and the shared-key writeback the KM consumes back out with `CMD_ABR_SK_TRANSFER`. Each lane is compared against a direct-register run of the same words, preceded by a run on different words. Also the value rule: the shared key the KM consumes, transferred to AES, is the engine's own `MLKEM_SHARED_KEY` words. A second writeback re-arms the notification: exactly one more `RESP_ABR_SHARED_KEY_READY` frame, with 1 to 3 queued commands answered. The `NAME` words are logged, not graded (`OBS-ABR-MLKEM-NAME`).
|<<sep_km_abr_mldsa_kat_test,`sep_km_abr_mldsa_kat_test`>>
|2
|no_cpu, real fuse sense
|ocah_axi_vip

|<<sep_km_sideload_share_walk_test,`sep_km_sideload_share_walk_test`>>
|2

Between the rounds one engine shred clears all eight, and each consumer then shows its documented invalid-key behaviour.

With the timeout disabled the read stays pending until the sequenced KM reset cancels it, and the warm path reads the sampler RDL resets. The same budget completes a read once the chain delivers.

The KM CPU wrapper decodes the range only under the simulation-only `OCAH_KM_VROM` define, which SEP DV does not set, so the load reaches the AXI crossbar. `sep_km_crossbar_efuse_window_rand_test` grades a load and a store at the base and a store at a drawn word of the window as DECERR.

|Proves the order of two frame faults, that the sequence counters wrap and restart after an overflow, a SEP flush and a warm reset, that the recoverable gate admits five commands, refuses the others after frame validation and holds until the acknowledge or a warm reset, and that an overflow with responses pending sends the fault frame first. The vault no-change check reads the taps.

|Proves that a fatal source makes the ROM shred, flush, report the first source in ISR order (the forced {drbg_err, axi_slverr} set reports -8), shred SRAM and trap, that the real wipe sets IRQ_STATUS.WIPE_STATE, that RESP_ABR_SHARED_KEY_READY carries no payload, that the CPU lines report -9 and -10 above every KMCSR source, that an organic DRBG stall with a key loaded in an engine reports -8, that with the ROM wipe off the engine key-valid vector (`KM-VALID`) in the clock of the bit-30 rise of that stall equals the vector after the key transfer, so the ROM fatal path does not shred the sideload banks (CHK-DRBG-STALL-KEYS), that a key write to an isolated path is fatal with code -7 (axi_slverr) and stops the transfer, that a main-context fatal fault sends one report, that a corrupt key read from the KPV gives the key_slot_crc fault and the aborted transfer writes no key bank and changes no key_valid bit (CHK-KEYCRC-OUTSIDE-NOWRITE), that every point with keys starts with key_valid equal to the transfer mask (CHK-PRE-KEYVALID), and that with the ROM wipe off the wipe pulse alone clears the sideload keys and the pending ML-KEM shared key, so the CMD_ABR_SK_TRANSFER after the next boot fails.

The response flags of each read of a virtual UART or testbench word are logged (OBS-KMCSR-READ-RESP), and the software-written words that no master writes read their RDL reset (CHK-KMCSR-RESET-READ). Each VUART_STATUS field reads its golden, bit by bit: TX_READY 1, RX_VALID 0 and reserved 0 (RDL reset), and PRINT_ENABLE the testbench drive (`km_csr.rdl`: "Set by testbench"): 1 in the entry with `+VUART_PRINT` and 0 in the entry `sep_km_kmcsr_reset_rand_test_print_off` without it, so a bit 2 that reads a constant fails one of the two entries (CHK-KMCSR-VUART-STATUS). |no_cpu, POKE, real fuse sense, HK-OTP
|KM bus operation image with result words, secure test mode and fuse sense probes, host mailbox master, host life-cycle path and host eFuse program path, `HK-OTP` rail flip inject with its `km_otp_flip_active_o` readback (added hook)
|Proves that the OTP readout equals the staged fuse image in dual-rail form, that it reads zero under the read locks (the response of a locked read is logged, OBS-OTP-LOCK-RESP), and in secure test mode, that a single-rail flip sets OTP_SIGINT, that a two-rail change sets OTP_CHANGE and, on the held point, leaves the readout word equal to its value before the flip after the release, that programmed fuse fields outside the KM interface do not reach the readout, that OTP_DEMOTION_STATE gives each group as `{~demote, demote}` (0xA at boot and after a SEP cold reset, 2'b01 in the group of each DEMOTE write), that IRQ_STATUS.OTP_SIGINT reads 0 after each KM reset, and that the reserved bits of the read registers read 0.

|<<sep_km_key_bank_register_rand_test,`sep_km_key_bank_register_rand_test`>>
|3
|no_cpu, POKE, +skip_fuse_sense
|KM bus operation image with result words, host mailbox master
|Proves that key-share registers read zero, that key_valid follows its set and clear rules, and that the ABR shared-key valid bit is set only by hardware and gates its interrupt. After a Key Manager wipe, one written share delivers its value, because the other share reads zero: the AES-256 ECB output equals the golden of the written key for each share written alone, with `KEY_VALID` 1 before the wipe, 0 after it and 1 after the write (CHK-SINGLE-SHARE; `hw/sys/sep/doc/crypto.adoc:192-196`; `hw/ip/key_manager/doc/architecture.adoc:507-509`). CTRL-SS-BOTH-SHARES shows that the engine consumes both shares. The entry `sep_km_key_bank_register_rand_test_shard2` (`+sep_shard=2`) grades the consume and the refused set on key_valid (CHK-BANK-SK-CONSUME), logs the key words instead of CHK-BANK-SK-SW, and runs the single-share leg; both entries run with `+esrc_noise_force`.

|<<sep_km_sram_scrambler_key_rand_test,`sep_km_sram_scrambler_key_rand_test`>>
|3
|no_cpu, IMG-SCR, +skip_fuse_sense
|KM scrambler image with a preloaded parameter block (`HK-IMG-PARAM`) and mailbox report, KM SRAM write counter, scrambled SRAM write monitor and `KM-SRAM` observation tap
|Proves that the stored form of an SRAM word depends on key and address, that the old key reads its data back after a key change, that a key write after the lock is ignored, and that a lock set with the enable clear keeps the scrambler on.
|===

=== Test Procedures

Unless a procedure says otherwise, each test starts from the common
SEP bring-up for its run mode (`bring_up_no_cpu`,
`bring_up_and_wait_fuse_sense`, or `boot_firmware`), drives stimulus
through the agents above, and ends with zero UVM/scoreboard errors
plus the checkers listed here.

Each test entry carries these labelled fields, in this order: *Phase*,
*Area*, *Run mode*, *Source Module*, *BFM / Model*, *Randomization* (where
the entry states one), *Objective*, *Test Coverage*, *Test Procedure*,
*Checkers* and *Pass Criteria*. Optional notes (*Evidence*, *Anti-vacuity*,
*Scope*, *Not claimed* and others) follow *Pass Criteria*. *Area* is the
Test Summary table that lists the test. *Test Coverage* is one line: the cells
or path walked. *Pass Criteria* is that every named checker PASSes and
`UVM_ERROR` is 0. The checker table is the fail-able contract.

Some records come from shared bring-up code and the shared sequences, and an
entry does not repeat them. Each one fails the leaf that runs it:

* `CHK-FW-IDENTITY` (every firmware leaf): the loaded TCM images carry the
  `FW-BUILD-ID` of the firmware source at the run's commit.
* `CHK-FW-CONSOLE` (every firmware leaf): the firmware mailbox reports
  completion with the PASS magic.
* `CHK-ALERTS-ZERO` (leaves that bring up real entropy): CSRNG and EDN
  `ERR_CODE` and `RECOV_ALERT` read 0 at the end of the run.
* `CHK-NOISE-FORCE` (leaves that hold the ESRC noise constant):
  `+esrc_noise_force` routes `esrc_noise_ext_i` to lane 0, shown by driving
  both values.
* `CHK-DONE-RW1C` and `CHK-ERR` (every KMAC operation of the KMAC sequence):
  `INTR_STATE.kmac_done` sets after absorb and clears by W1C, and `ERR_CODE`
  and `INTR_STATE.kmac_err` read 0.

include::test_contracts/smoke_uvm.adoc[tag=smoke]

include::test_contracts/key_manager.adoc[]

'''''

[[sep-vplan-phase3]]
=== Phase 3 Tests

Phase 3 tests prove the residual contracts of each area (<<sep-vplan-phase3-overview>>).
Each area has its Phase 3 summary rows in its table of the
Scenario-Expanded Test Summary, and its Phase 3 entries at the end of its
section of Test Procedures, after the entries of Phase 1 and Phase 2. The rows
are for human readers and the entries are for tools and audits. The two name
the same tests. An entry follows the field rules of Test Procedures and adds the fields
that its area section states. A Phase 3 test never overlaps a Phase 1 or
Phase 2 test. An entry that extends an open test names that test and the cell
that the entry adds. The Phase 3 contracts that no test can grade are in
<<sep-vplan-phase3-lists>>. The randomization class of every test is in
<<sep-vplan-phase3-random>>. Each Phase 3 leaf opens the coverage window of its
own entry only around its graded stimulus (<<sep-fcov-graded-window,SEP_FCOV>>).
The window gate is a testbench variable that only the coverage sampler reads; it
drives nothing and touches no DUT net. The sampler `u_sep_fcov` exists only on
VCS, so a Verilator leaf skips the gate write and runs the same checks.
Its bring-up, its control legs and a reset sequence between legs run with the
window closed, so they fill no Phase 3 bin, and neither does the traffic of
another test.

[[sep-vplan-phase3-overview]]
==== Phase 3 Overview

[cols="3,2,8",options="header"]
|===
|Area |Test numbers |Scope
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|1.25 to 1.55, 1.60
|Crypto interrupt lines and cross-engine concurrency (two extension legs of open tests), HMAC, AES, KMAC, OTBN, ABR, delivered-key select, crypto alerts, ESRC health, FIFO integrity, autotune and conditioning, DRBG faults and sinks, TRNG stream handshake and external entropy to the Key Manager.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-fabric-remap,Fabric and remap>>
|2.25 to 2.33
|Inbound and outbound filter match with granule, default deny and source ID, global-to-local rebase, outbound route and output remap, alias remap, SMC route, extension port window, row responses with burst refusal, and DMA endpoint reach.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-mailbox-irq-pic,Mailbox, IRQ, PIC>>
|3.13 to 3.24
|Mailbox lines, FIFOs, errors, filter types and reset; PIC registers, priority, gateway cells, source map and external inputs; bridge fault latches and the fault-versus-clear race; timeout registers; NMI vector; timer and software interrupt tie-offs.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-spi-dma,SPI and DMA>>
|4.7 to 4.20
|SPI pad, lane data at x1, x2 and x4, output-enable, FIFO, interrupt, error, strobe and reset contracts (one entry extends an open SPI test), and the SPI lane coverage statement; DMA address modes, geometry, interrupt gate, range lock, abort, inline hash and SPI handshake.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-mem-boot-reset,Memory, boot, reset, WDT, clock>>
|5.13 to 5.32
|Reset release, cold and watchdog reset, software reset and isolation, JTAG and scan overrides, reset vector and run request, `SECURE_TM`, state held through a scan session, `test_en_i` reach to the test inputs, scratch and CPU control registers, watchdog, wakeup timer and reference counter, memory apertures and ECC, CPU demux, debug master, ISA and PMP.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-efuse-lifecycle,eFuse, lifecycle, SEC_DIS, locked-field>>
|6.18 to 6.30
|eFuse windows and fields, lifecycle code words, feature control, demotion and DFT gate ports, transient RMA, token channels, faults and latch freeze, interface status, lock slots, secure test mode, JTAG access, SEC_DIS timing, sense response and program interface faults.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-key-manager,Key Manager vault>>
|7.22 to 7.37
|Command framing, recovery and arguments; key slots, handles and allocator capacity; engine delivery and transfer policy; ABR shared key; boot and cold domain; SRAM load and handover; fault reports; KMCSR, SRAM lock and reset domains; KPV; CPU trap and execute protection; crossbar and eFuse window; KM-side mailbox; DRBG sampler; OTP interface; engine key registers; SRAM scrambler.
|===

[[sep-vplan-phase3-parked]]
==== Phase 3 Parked Tests

A parked test has no graded check that runs without a PROPOSED item or an
unstated value (<<sep-vplan-credit-rules>>). Its entry carries the field
`*Needs*:`, its summary row reads "3, parked", it takes no credit, it is not in
an executable total, and its bins are blocked bins of `SEP_FCOV.adoc`
(<<sep-fcov-parked,Phase 2 Planned Groups>>).

No Phase 3 entry carries a `*Needs*:` field, so every Phase 3 test is
executable: at least one of its graded checks is not blocked. An entry names
the PROPOSED item or the unstated value of each of its blocked checks and legs.


[[sep-vplan-phase3-random]]
==== Phase 3 Randomization

Every Phase 3 stimulus that has a legal matrix is a pure function of the run
seed, and `--stage sim --seed N` replays the leaf. The stream is not a CSPRNG
and no drawn value is a key, a token or a nonce. No document quotes a per-seed
value as evidence. The class of a test is one of:

* `RAND-REP`: the seed draws points from a legal matrix, and every point has a stated outcome.
* `RANDCFG`: the seed draws a configuration, the test runs one scenario under it, and the expected outcome is a function of the configuration.
* `RAND-NONE`: the stimulus is directed, for the reason the entry states.

[[sep-vplan-phase3-drawn]]
*Drawn cells.* Regression seeds are random per leaf, and a leaf does not know
its run index, so no seed and no testlist entry pins a cell. A leaf draws its
cells from its seed (`SepSeededRng`, weighted toward the corners where the entry
says so, with the constraint filters of the entry). A leaf walks a cell set in
every run only when the set is cheap and contract-critical: a legal minimum or
maximum, one representative of each leg, or the error-injection case itself,
with no extra boot, no extra cold reset and no extra transfer of real size per
cell. The coverage bins of such cells fill in one `all --cov` regression. The
coverage bins of drawn cells are seed-dependent and close over a merge of
regressions, never in one regression (<<sep-fcov-rand-phase2,SEP_FCOV>>). For N
equally likely cells and d draws per leaf the expected closure is about
N * ln(N) / (d * reseed) regressions. The seed count of an entry stays small
(3 or 4) and is not raised to fill cells; an entry that runs more seeds states
the reason. The eFuse secure test mode matrix entry (6.27) runs 9 seeds: its
drawn state has five equal-weight values, and 9 seeds draw each of PROD_END and
RMA_CHIPLET over merged regressions. An entry whose leaf runs its whole cell set in every run, so that
the seed selects only orders, values and timing, runs one seed per regression
(no `reseed`); each regression draws a new seed, so those values still vary.

*Testlist entries.* A Phase 3 test has one testlist entry. Each entry runs on
its testlist target, so a test that grades some checks on one simulator only
splits them inside its one entry, and the leaf selects them from the simulator.
Full-CPU legs of a `no_cpu` test run in a dedicated full-CPU entry
(`target = "default"`, `+sep_require_full_cpu`, which fails a leaf on a CPU-stub
build), and the leaf selects them from the build it runs on. A VCS `--cov` run
uses `--target default` for every entry.
A second entry exists only when the run mode differs in a way that one entry
cannot carry: a time-0 bench plusarg that the prestage hook cannot set, a
different run mode (`cpu` and `no_cpu`), or a full-CPU leg. No entry exists to pin a cell (no
per-cell variant, no cell or field plusarg). The tests with a second entry are
5.13 `sep_boot_release_terms_test`, whose entry
`sep_boot_release_terms_test_smc_hold` adds the bench plusarg
`+sep_smc_fuse_sense_hold`; 5.19 `sep_cpu_reset_entry_test`, whose entry
`sep_cpu_reset_entry_test_real_sense` omits the time-0 plusarg
`+skip_fuse_sense` and runs real fuse sense; 5.28 `sep_debug_master_target_matrix_test`, which
has one `cpu` entry and one `no_cpu` entry
(`sep_debug_master_target_matrix_test_sba`, on `target = "default"`) of the one
module; 5.15 `sep_cpu_reset_retention_test`, 5.20
`sep_secure_tm_strap_sampling_test` and 5.22 `sep_timer_clock_contract_test`,
whose full-CPU entries `sep_cpu_reset_retention_test_dbg`,
`sep_secure_tm_strap_sampling_test_dbg` and `sep_timer_clock_contract_test_dbg`
run the `dbg_rstb_i` legs on VCS; and 7.28
`sep_km_fault_inject_rand_test`, which has one entry for each of its two
preloaded `rom_main` builds (HOST and HOST-UNREC, set by the t=0 plusarg
`+km_rom_hex`), and whose HOST-UNREC build the regression runs as fourteen
shard entries (`sep_km_fault_inject_rand_test_unrec_shard1` to `_shard14`). An extension leg runs in the testlist entry of its
open test and adds no entry. Where the leg needs more seeds than that entry
runs, the entry takes the seed count of the leg (1.26).

[[sep-vplan-phase3-edn-waiver]]
*JTAG reset override waiver.* The JTAG `sep_reset_n` override and the per-IP
JTAG reset holds reset the crypto engines without the isolation sequence, so an
engine can drop an ungranted EDN request with no endpoint cancel, and the
`tb_top` crypto EDN request-hold assertions fire on a compliant DUT. Every
testlist entry whose leaf drives the `sep_reset_n` override (on the bench pins
`jtag_sep_reset_n_ovrd_i` / `jtag_sep_reset_n_val_i` or through the IC_RESET
TDR) or a per-IP hold `jtag_<ip>_rst_hold_i` adds `+sep_edn_jtag_reset_waive`.
The plusarg turns off only the crypto EDN arbiter and adapter request-hold
assertions (`tb/tb_top.sv`), and `tb_top` logs that it is set. The Phase 3
entries that add it are 5.17, 5.18, 5.20, 5.22, 5.31 and 6.29. Entries 5.18 and 5.31 add it
because the scan reset asserts the same crypto engine resets without the
isolation sequence.

A test with two classes uses two of the three classes on different legs: a
drawn configuration for one leg and a matrix point for another, or a directed
leg next to a drawn leg. The entry names the dimensions of each class, and the
table below gives both classes of such a test. A row marked "extension" or
"extension leg" names an open test. Its class is the class of the cells that
the entry adds. A row marked "parked" names a parked test
(<<sep-vplan-phase3-parked>>).

[[sep-vplan-phase3-lists]]
==== Phase 3 Blocked Features, Observation Gaps and Scope

Each item is listed once. The area column names every area that the item
concerns. A blocked feature carries no Phase 3 credit, and random draws exclude
one. A feature with no observation point has no check that can fail on it. An
out of scope feature is graded by an open Phase 1 or Phase 2 test, or has no
stated pass or fail value, or belongs to another area. A PROPOSED internal
drive, an added hook and an observation tap that a test adds are listed in
<<sep-vplan-phase3-proposed>>. A check that needs a PROPOSED item is blocked
(<<sep-vplan-credit-rules>>). A check that needs only observation taps and added
hooks is not blocked.

[[sep-vplan-phase3-blocked]]
===== Blocked features

A blocked feature has no check in Phase 3, because the specification or the
register description does not state the value that a check needs. The
question names what the specification must state before a check can fail on
the feature. Where a test uses part of a blocked feature, the note says what
the test does.

[cols="3,5,6,4",options="header"]
|===
|Area |Feature |Open specification question |Note
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|`STATUS.hmac_idle` reports that HMAC is idle; the register text gives only its reset value 1 and the rule that `DIGEST` and `MSG_LENGTH` writes are legal while it is 1.
|HMAC `STATUS.hmac_idle`: what value does the bit take during a hash, after `hmac_done`, and after a start that `CFG` blocks? Source: `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/hmac/data/hmac.hjson` (`STATUS.hmac_idle`); `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/hmac/regs/gen/adoc/hmac.adoc` (`STATUS`).
|`sep_hmac_illegal_cfg_start_test` and `sep_hmac_fifo_stream_rand_test` log the bit as `OBS-HMAC-IDLE` and do not grade it. `sep_hmac_swap_context_rand_test` waits for the bit before a context write, as the register text states.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|`hw/sys/sep/doc/aes.adoc` lists the key sizes 128, 192 and 256 and the modes ECB, CBC, CFB, OFB, CTR and CTR-GCM as supported. The SEP build has GCM support, so `CTRL_GCM_SHADOWED.PHASE`, `NUM_VALID_BYTES` and the `AES_GCM` mode code take the values the register text states for a GCM build.
|No specification question: `hw/sys/sep/doc/aes.adoc` (AES Cipher) lists CTR-GCM, its OCAH Modifications change only the bus interfaces, and the OpenTitan default of `AESGCMEnable` is 1 (`vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/aes/data/aes.hjson`, `AESGCMEnable`), so the clauses for a build with GCM disabled at compile time do not apply. Source: `hw/sys/sep/doc/aes.adoc`; `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/aes/data/aes.hjson` (`AESGCMEnable`, `CTRL_GCM_SHADOWED`); `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/aes/doc/programmers_guide.md` (GCM Encryption, GCM Decryption).
|`sep_aes_cfb_ofb_gcm_rand_test` grades GCM encryption and decryption (`MODE` = `AES_GCM`, the phase sequence of the programmer's guide) against the NIST SP 800-38D golden of the ciphertext and the tag.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|CTRL_AUX_SHADOWED.FORCE_MASKS needs the Masking and SecAllowForcingMasks parameters; the OCAH build values are not stated.
|AES: values of the Masking and SecAllowForcingMasks parameters in the OCAH build. Source: `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/aes/regs/gen/adoc/aes.adoc` (`CTRL_AUX_SHADOWED`).
|No test logs it.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|KMAC CFG_SHADOWED.mode selects SHA3, SHAKE or cSHAKE. The generated register text gives the field and its reset value 0; the codes come from the upstream register description named in the Conventions. The behavior of the fourth code is not stated.
|KMAC: behaviour of the fourth CFG_SHADOWED.mode code. Source: `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/kmac/regs/gen/adoc/kmac.adoc` (`CFG_SHADOWED`).
|`sep_kmac_unsupported_cfg_inject_test` drives mode 2'b01 with `en_unsupported_modestrength` = 0 and grades the error (CHK-KMAC-UNSUP, CHK-KMAC-ERRCODE). With the bit set it grades the absorb and the error at start (CHK-KMAC-UNSUP-EN, `kmac.adoc:114-115`, `theory_of_operation.md:785`) and logs the end state of the run as `OBS-KMAC-MODE01` as an open question.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|KMAC ERR_CODE holds a code in [31:24] and debug data in [23:0]. The code values are stated (`vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/kmac/doc/theory_of_operation.md:720-731`); the debug data has no stated value.
|KMAC: what value do bits [23:0] of ERR_CODE hold for each code? Source: `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/kmac/regs/gen/adoc/kmac.adoc` (`ERR_CODE`).
|`sep_kmac_unsupported_cfg_inject_test` grades the reset value 0 before the start and `ERR_CODE[31:24]` = 0x06 after each blocked start (CHK-KMAC-ERRCODE) and masks [23:0]. `sep_kmac_entropy_mode_rand_test` grades the reset value 0 before each EDN wait-timer run (`CTRL-KMAC-TIMER-PRESTATE`) and `ERR_CODE[31:24]` = 0x04 after each wait-timer expiry (CHK-KMAC-ERRCODE-ENTROPY, `theory_of_operation.md:724`, `:764`), masks [23:0] and logs them (`OBS-KMAC-TIMER-ERRCODE`); CHK-KMAC-TIMER grades `kmac_err`
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|Firmware must zeroize between back-to-back ABR operations. The hardware response to a start without zeroize is not stated.
|ABR: hardware response to a start without a zeroize. Source: `hw/sys/sep/doc/adams_bridge.adoc` (OCAH Modifications).
|`sep_abr_command_matrix_rand_test` (CHK-ABR-PARKED-CMD) writes a command to the `CTRL` of a parked block with no `ZEROIZE` bit and grades that the command does not run and that `STATUS` keeps `VALID` 1 and `READY` 0 (`abr_reg.rdl:77`, `swwe = abr_ready`). The write must answer OKAY, and `STATUS.ERROR` is logged only.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|ABR sets IRQ_STATUS.KEY_VALID on the final shared-key word and raises a gated interrupt to the Key Manager. The gating control and the KM handshake are not stated.
|ABR: gating control and Key Manager handshake of the shared-key interrupt. Source: `hw/sys/sep/doc/adams_bridge.adoc` (OCAH Modifications).
|No test logs it.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|DECORRELATOR_CTRL.BYPASS (per lane) forces the feedback to zero. SAMPLE_CLK_DIV sets the byte period to SAMPLE_CLK_DIV + 1 cycles. The RDL names sample-clock cycles and the architecture text names clk_i cycles.
|ESRC: time base of the byte period (RDL: sample-clock cycles; architecture text: clk_i cycles). Source: `hw/ip/entropy_source/regs/gen/adoc/entropy_source.adoc` (`DECORRELATOR_CTRL`); `hw/ip/entropy_source/doc/architecture.adoc` (Decorrelation and BIW Mixing).
|no check uses an absolute period; the exact noise patterns change a lane bit only at a strobe of `esrc_decor_valid_o`, so their statistics count words and not clocks. `sep_esrc_conditioning_matrix_rand_test` grades only the ratio of two periods (CHK-COND-PERIOD-RATIO) and logs the period in `clk_i` cycles as `OBS-COND-PERIOD-ABS`
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|The gating 32-bit health test observes the extracted stream before SHA-256. The whitener conditions each 512-bit BIW block into a 256-bit digest. The block chaining, the padding, and the word and byte order of the input and digest words are not stated.
|ESRC SHA-256 whitener: does each block start from the SHA-256 initial value or from the previous digest, is the block padded, and in which word and byte order do the 16 input words enter the block and the 8 digest words enter the FIFO? Source: `hw/ip/entropy_source/doc/architecture.adoc` (SHA-256 Whitening); `hw/ip/entropy_source/regs/gen/adoc/entropy_source.adoc` (`CTRL.SHA256_WHITENING_ENABLE`); `hw/sys/sep/doc/trng.adoc` (Entropy Generation).
|`sep_esrc_health_pattern_rand_test` grades the exact 16-to-8 word count of the whitener on a constant-zero stream, logs the digest words, and does not grade the position of the 32-bit test. `sep_esrc_health_pattern_rand_test` logs `OBS-HT-PRESHA-WORDS` as an open question.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|HEALTH_TEST_STATUS latches repetition [0], APT [3], Markov high [4] and Markov low [5]. Bits [2:1] and [7:6] and the APT high/low split are not stated.
|ESRC: meaning of HEALTH_TEST_STATUS bits 1, 2, 6 and 7 and the split of the adaptive-proportion failure into high and low. Source: `hw/ip/entropy_source/regs/gen/adoc/entropy_source.adoc` (`HEALTH_TEST_STATUS`); `hw/ip/entropy_source/doc/architecture.adoc` (Health Testing).
|`sep_esrc_health_pattern_rand_test` grades bit 3 as a trip on a biased lane, bits 0, 4 and 5 as trips by a limit beyond the exact statistics of a `square` or `alternate` stream and as pass cells, and bits 3 and 5 on a constant-zero 32-bit stream, and bit 3 with `APT_HI_TOTAL_FAILS` on a constant all-ones 32-bit stream. `sep_esrc_alert_delivery_test` logs `OBS-HT-RSVD` as an open question.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|The 32-bit health test with `CTRL.BYPASS_ENTROPY_COMPRESSOR` set. `architecture.adoc` (Entropy Source) states that the test is clock-gated off in that mode; `architecture.adoc` (Health Testing) states that the 32-bit instance monitors the post-BIW (or post-bypass) stream.
|ESRC: with the compressor bypassed, is the 32-bit health test off, or does it test the bypass words? Source: `hw/ip/entropy_source/doc/architecture.adoc` (Entropy Source; Health Testing).
|`sep_esrc_conditioning_matrix_rand_test` runs a control trial and a bypass trial with stuck0 and logs `HEALTH_TEST_STATUS` as `OBS-BLOCKED CHK-COND-HT-OFF`; nothing is graded
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|MAIN_SM_STATUS.STATE is a 9-bit sparse state. The value list is not stated.

|`sep_esrc_startup_gate_rand_test` logs `OBS-ESRC-SM-STATE` as an open question.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|MIN_ENTROPY_H.H is Q4.4 (reset 0xC). `entropy_source.rdl` derives both advisory cutoffs per SP 800-90B, `RCT_LIMIT` as C = 1 + ceil(20 / H) and `APT_LIMIT` for a 1024-sample window. The H = 0 result is not stated.
|ESRC: the result for H = 0. Source: `hw/ip/entropy_source/regs/gen/adoc/entropy_source.adoc` (`MIN_ENTROPY_H`, `RECOMMENDED_THRESHOLDS`).
|`sep_esrc_fips_lock_test` grades `RCT_LIMIT` against C = 1 + ceil(20 / H) and `APT_LIMIT` against the SP 800-90B 4.4.2 binomial cutoff (W = 1024, alpha = 2^-20) for every H above 0. It logs both limits at H = 0 as `OBS-REC-THRESH-H0` and does not grade them.

|`sep_drbg_cmd_sequence_rand_test` logs `OBS-DRBG-MAIN-SM` as an open question.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|CSRNG CTRL.ENABLE and EDN CTRL.EDN_ENABLE carry an order rule that points to the Programmer's Guide. The disable order and the hardware response to a wrong order are not stated.

|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>

|The KM stream has no post-mux adapter and is not scrubbed. A mid-word TRNG reset can mix old and new bytes or raise a stream error. Which one occurs is not stated.

|no stream traffic on the Key Manager path during a reset. No test logs it.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-mailbox-irq-pic,Mailbox, IRQ, PIC>>

|Mailbox to SMC port bit: is mailbox `m` on port bit `m` or on bit `7 - m`? `mailbox.adoc` words the SMC numbers 7 to 0 and the unit document words them 0 to 7: which wording is the numbering of the port bits? |vector bit 15 is logged and not graded. `sep_esrc_csr_rw_walk_rand_test` logs `OBS-PIC16` as an open question. `sep_esrc_persistent_alert_rand_test` grades one cause only, the persistent failure with every defined enable set (CHK-PIC16).
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>; <<sep-vplan-phase3-mem-boot-reset,Memory, boot, reset>>
|SEP_TEST_CTRL.fast_pka_en is a read-only bit (reset 0). What it changes and what drives it are not stated.

|`sep_address_map_test` logs `OBS-SEP-TEST-CTRL` as an open question.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>; <<sep-vplan-phase3-mem-boot-reset,Memory, boot, reset>>
|SEP_CPU_CTRL.PKA_CTRL has three RW placeholder fields marked "not yet implemented". Whether they must have no effect is not stated.
|PKA_CTRL placeholder fields: must they have no effect? What do the `PKA_CTRL` DPA and noise-source bits change? Source: `hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/blocks/sep_cpu_ctrl.adoc` (`PKA_CTRL`).
|No test logs it.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>
|SEP uses three AXI-Stream DRBG outputs, no direct native EDN endpoints and 8-entry endpoint FIFOs. The instantiated seed FIFO depth is not stated.

|no check uses a depth. `sep_drbg_cmd_sequence_rand_test` logs `OBS-DRBG-SEED-INGRESS` (the accepted words before the first dropped word) as an open question.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy,Crypto and entropy>>

|DMA and CPU debug not-connected response
|What bus response code does the DMA receive on its not-connected cells (DMA CSR, entropy pool, boot ROM), and what does the CPU debug master receive on its not-connected cells (boot ROM, CPU TCM)? No DMA path reaches these targets (`hw/sys/sep/doc/assets/sep_axi_connectivity.svg`), and a bus error response ends the transfer with `STATUS.ERROR` and `ERROR_CODE.BUS_ERROR` (`secure_dma.adoc`; OpenTitan DMA `programmers_guide.md`, "Error Condition"), but no spec sentence states that the fabric answers such a request with an error.
|`sep_fabric_dma_endpoint_matrix_test` (CHK-DMA-NOTCONN) grades that the DMA issues the request at the target (the DMA master tap `dma_axi_req_probe_o` shows the AR or AW), that the request does not reach the target and that each run ends with a DMA interrupt; the end state (`STATUS`, `ERROR_CODE`) is logged as `OBS-DMA-NOTCONN-RESP`. The rom_dst leg grades the PR-ROM silence and the dma_csr_src leg grades the PR-DMACSR silence, and PR-ROM and PR-DMACSR are observation taps the test adds to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list. The pool_src leg needs a pool level of 1 or more and below the 32-entry depth before the GO (precondition `CTRL-DMA-POOL-PRE`; the test sends fewer word pairs than the depth on the external stream 2), stops the pool fill before the GO, shows the DMA read on the DMA master tap `dma_axi_req_probe_o`, keeps the staged SRAM word and leaves the level equal to the level before the GO. A further run uses the PIC base as the source; its end state and destination are logged as `OBS-DMA-NOTCONN-RESP` (`tgt=pic_src`) with no verdict. The Key Manager mailbox `STATUS` word is reachable (`sep_axi_connectivity.svg:130`) and its copy is graded by CHK-DMA-KM-SRC.

|<<sep-vplan-phase3-spi-dma,SPI and DMA>>
|SPI FIFO depths
|`spi.adoc` (Features) states no depth number. The SEP SPI host is the unmodified OpenTitan IP, whose parameters are TxDepth 72, RxDepth 64 and CmdDepth 4 (`spi_host.hjson:93-107`; `sep-programming.adoc:811`), and the SEP build keeps them. At which CMDQD does READY stay 0, and how many RX words land past RXFULL?
|Graded leg: TX 73 words before TXFULL, RXFULL and RXQD 64 at an idle 64-word Rx segment (`sep_spi_fifo_stall_watermark_rand_test`, CHK-SPI-DEPTH-TX, CHK-SPI-DEPTH-RX). CMDQD at 4 or below while the leaf writes COMMANDs behind a held segment, each after a STATUS read with READY=1, until READY stays 0 for 20000 core clocks (CHK-SPI-CMDQD-DEPTH; CmdDepth 4, `spi_host.hjson:103-106`, CMDQD `spi_host.hjson:318-319`). The RX words past RXFULL, the CMDQD at which READY stays 0 and the head-segment count are logged (`OBS-SPI-RX-EXTRA`, `OBS-SPI-CMDQD-FULL`): `theory_of_operation.md:620`, `:373` and `programmers_guide.md:143-144` give no single point at which READY stays 0. Gated leg: the RX watermark crossing at the reset value 0x7F (the FCOV Bins-not-graded table, `cp_rxwm_reset`)
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-spi-dma,SPI and DMA>>

(`vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/spi_host/doc/theory_of_operation.md:597`). `spi_host.hjson` (`ERROR_ENABLE.CSIDINVAL`) raises the error when CSID exceeds NumCS. `sep_io` has one chip select (`spi.adoc`, Features: `NUM_CS = 1`).
|Graded leg: CSID from 2 to 0xFFFFFFFE (`sep_spi_ot_host_csr_irq_rand_test`, CHK-SPI-CSID-RANGE), where the statements agree. CSID=1 is logged as `OBS-SPI-CSID1 LOG`

A start with a reserved ASID (not 0x7, 0x9 or 0xa) ends with STATUS.ERROR=1 and ERROR_CODE.ASID_ERROR=1 and no DMA AR or AW beat (CHK-DMA-ASID-ERR; `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/dma/doc/registers.md:254-274`; `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/secure_dma/regs/gen/adoc/secure_dma.adoc:342`)

|How does a forced DMA interrupt release (`secure_dma.adoc`, INTR_STATE, INTR_TEST)? `secure_dma.adoc` and `secure_dma.rdl` give INTR_STATE as read-only and `dma.hjson` types the interrupts "status". A write of 1 to the matching STATUS bit releases a status interrupt that the hardware set ("Since interrupts are implemented as status bits, they are cleared by writing a '1' to the corresponding bit in the STATUS register", OpenTitan DMA `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/dma/doc/programmers_guide.md:39`; `dma.adoc:131-132`); no write that releases a bit forced by `INTR_TEST` is stated.
|Graded leg: Enable gate and event lines, the single-chunk copy, and the release of INTR_STATE.DMA_ERROR by a write of 1 to STATUS.ERROR (`sep_dma_irq_error_lock_rand_test`, CHK-DMA-IRQ, CHK-DMA-IRQ-SINGLE, CHK-DMA-INTR-RELEASE). The release of DMA_DONE after DONE and of DMA_CHUNK_DONE after a non-final CHUNK_DONE by a write of 1 to the STATUS bit is graded by the same checker. Interrupt trials start from a cold reset. No test logs it.

|Group ID matching is disabled at build time, so `group_id` (bits 23:20 of `FILTER_CONFIG`) has no effect on any verdict. |Which transaction field carries the `group_id` that the filter compares with the entry field, and can a bench master drive a non-zero value? The entry does not carry it.

|Software write class of fields with register access R
|Is a shadow field that the register description gives access R software-writable (field table) or read-only (register description)? `LC_STATE` has access R in the register description, and the field table gives it per-bit set-only rules with token gates. Phase 3 frontdoor writes exclude `LC_STATE`, so the per-bit rules of that write, its token gates, its terminal states, its lock exemption and its refusal under secure test mode or a comparator fault carry no grade. The same conflict holds for `BL1_VERSION` and `BL2_VERSION`: access R in `hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/blocks/sep_efuse_map.adoc` (address map rows `0x8C` and `0xAC`), set-only in the field table of `hw/sys/sep/doc/otp_fuse_controller.adoc`. Reads of these fields carry no grade: `sep_efuse_window_decode_reset_test` takes its control shadow write on `SYS_DIS`, and `sep_km_otp_interface_rand_test` writes `REQUIRED_ALGS` and `REQUIRED_SIGNERS`. Every shadow write of the Phase 3 tests goes to a field with access RW: `LOCKS`, `LOCKS_SPARE`, `SIP_DIS`, `SYS_DIS`, `REQUIRED_ALGS` and `REQUIRED_SIGNERS`.

|Which signal is test enable and what does the freeze do to a match in progress?
|A match held into `SECURE_TM` = 1 is graded by `sep_secure_tm_strap_sampling_test` (CHK-STM-SECDIS-FEAT), and a match held through a scan session by `sep_scan_mode_state_hold_test` (CHK-SCAN-SECDIS-HOLD). Every SEC_DIS token go runs with `secure_tm_req_i` at 0, except the leg-E go of `sep_secure_tm_strap_sampling_test`: CHK-STM-LATE needs the strap at 1 when the token arrives, so that a DUT that samples the strap at the token fails. |Does a frontdoor `LC_STATE` shadow write change the state? `hw/sys/sep/regs/blocks/sep_efuse_map/sep_efuse_map.rdl` (register `LC_STATE`) gives `lc_state` [7:0] and `rsvd` [31:8] as `sw = r`; `hw/sys/sep/doc/otp_fuse_controller.adoc` (Fuse Fields) gives `LC_STATE` as SW writable set-only and says explicit shadow writes remain subject to token and transition checks.
|`sep_lcc_lc_state_transition_matrix_test` grades every write against the fuse-field golden (set-only, token and transition checks) and logs bits [31:8] as `OBS-LC-UPPER`, an open question. `CTRL-LC-WR-LANDED` reads back a written `[31:8]` bit, so it depends on that open question.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-efuse-lifecycle,eFuse, lifecycle>>
|Write to demotion bits [63:2]
|The register description marks `DEMOTE_1` and `DEMOTE_2` bits [63:2] "Not used" and the specification states no effect for a write to them beyond the write-one-to-set storage of the register definition. `sep_lcc_feat_ctrl_state_matrix_rand_test` grades that each written bit reads back set and stays set until cold reset (`CHK-DEMOTE-STEP`, `hw/sys/sep/regs/blocks/sep_lifecycle_ctrl/sep_lifecycle_ctrl.rdl:40-45`) and that `FEAT_CTRL` stays the row of the cumulative demotion.
|No test logs the effect of a write to these bits on another output.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-efuse-lifecycle,eFuse, lifecycle>>
|Response of a `DEMOTE_1` or `DEMOTE_2` write
|The register description gives the demotion and lock bits as write-one-to-set and the specification states the demotion lock; neither states the bus response of a DEMOTE write, applied or refused by the lock.
|`sep_lcc_feat_ctrl_state_matrix_rand_test` grades each DEMOTE write by readback and `FEAT_CTRL` (`CHK-DEMOTE-STEP`, `CHK-FEAT-CELL`) and logs the write response as `OBS-LCC-WR-RESP`; `sep_lcc_state_codeword_rand_test` logs the response of its demotion writes the same way.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-efuse-lifecycle,eFuse, lifecycle>>
|Level of `sep_fuse_dft_disable_o` and `smc_fuse_dft_disable_o`
|`hw/sys/sep/doc/port_table.adoc` (`sep_fuse_dft_disable_o`, `smc_fuse_dft_disable_o`) composes each port as Case 3 AND `SEP_FUSE_DBG` (Case 2 AND `SMC_FUSE_DBG`). The port name says disable; the text does not say whether the port is high when the composed term holds or when it does not.
|`sep_lcc_demote_feat_ctrl_matrix_test` grades each port against the first cell of its class and that the open and closed classes differ (`CHK-FUSE-DFT-DIS`), and logs the level of each class once as `OBS-DTP-PORT-LEVEL`.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-efuse-lifecycle,eFuse, lifecycle>>
|`lc_sigint_err` after a complementary codeword returns
|`hw/sys/sep/doc/lifecycle_controller.adoc` (Life Cycle Controller) states that a non-complementary codeword raises `lc_sigint_err` and forces the feature control. It does not state whether the error and the forced feature control release when the codeword is complementary again before a reset.
|`sep_efuse_lcc_lc_state_stitch_test` logs `lc_sigint_err` and `FEAT_CTRL` two clocks after the injection ends as `OBS-LC-SIGINT-RELEASE`. `sep_lcc_state_codeword_rand_test` logs the same after its first epoch (`OBS-LC-SIGINT-RELEASE`). An epoch of it that finds the error still set first restores a clean start by a cold reset (`INFO-SIGINT-RESTORE`), so no check grades the release.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-efuse-lifecycle,eFuse, lifecycle>>
|Blocked CHIPLET match after a SiP match
|The specification does not say if a blocked CHIPLET match fires again after the SiP match sets bit 1. (`sep_efuse_transient_rma_rand_test` presents a CHIPLET mismatch go in between.)
|No test logs it.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-efuse-lifecycle,eFuse, lifecycle>>
|DFT fuse gate port polarity
|The polarity of `sep_fuse_dft_disable_o` and `smc_fuse_dft_disable_o` is not stated. (`sep_lcc_feat_ctrl_state_matrix_rand_test` names no level. It compares the two port values with each other by case class and grades the feature-control bit of each case against the inverse of its disable bit, which the specification states.)
|`sep_lcc_feat_ctrl_state_matrix_rand_test` logs `OBS-DTP-PORT-LEVEL` as an open question.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-efuse-lifecycle,eFuse, lifecycle>>
|CPU-side response at the eFuse interface-control holes and the reserved span
|What exact response code and read data does the SEP CPU side see past the interface-control extent (0x1093_041C to 0x1093_04FF)? The response class "not OKAY" there is graded from `hw/sys/sep/doc/otp_fuse_controller.adoc` (section "OTP Fuse Controller", APB decode SLVERR). What response does the SEP CPU side see for the reserved span 0x1093_0600 to 0x1093_FFFF? No source states it: `hw/sys/sep/doc/memory_map.adoc` gives DECERR at the inbound port, and `hw/sys/sep/doc/otp_fuse_controller.adoc` sends that span to the shim bank control registers. The read data of the reserved span is not stated either, because the span goes to the shim bank control registers, whose contents the adopter defines. It is logged and not graded.
|`sep_unmapped_access_policy_test` grades the response past the interface-control extent. `sep_efuse_window_decode_reset_test` logs the response and the read data and grades no change of `STATUS`, `PROGRAM_CTRL`, `READ_CTRL` or a shadow word. The test does not claim that a past-extent or reserved access never answers OKAY. `sep_efuse_window_decode_reset_test` logs `OBS-WIN-RESP` as an open question.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-efuse-lifecycle,eFuse, lifecycle>>
|eFuse guard on an out-of-range command address
|Does the eFuse guard drop a read or program command with an address of 8192 or more? The register description states only the status flags.
|`sep_efuse_interface_status_rand_test` grades the flags and logs the image difference. `sep_efuse_interface_status_rand_test` logs `OBS-IF-OOB-CMD` as an open question.
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-efuse-lifecycle,eFuse, lifecycle>>
|Program-request outcome at the program timeout
|What do `program_status`, `program_done` and `program_busy` report when the program timeout fires, and does the interface accept a new request? The register text gives the read-side status only. |`sep_efuse_shim_response_fault_test` logs the timeout-enabled cell (`OBS-PROG-TIMEOUT`) and does not grade it
|<<sep-vplan-phase3-efuse-lifecycle,eFuse, lifecycle>>
|Sense response status on lock, lifecycle, disable and last words
|The word itself is stated: a status leaves words 0 to 9 (`LOCKS`, `LOCKS_SPARE`, `LC_STATE`, `SBOOT_DIS`, `TRANSIENT_RMA_EN`, `SIP_DIS`, `SYS_DIS`) unwritten. The feature controls follow the profile row of those shadows. Is word 255 with a status left unwritten, or stored as the last word, as the next sentence says? Source: `hw/sys/sep/doc/otp_fuse_controller.adoc` (Fuse Sensing and Boot Sequencing).
|`sep_efuse_shim_response_fault_test` grades words 10 to 254 and a drawn control word of 0 to 9 (`CHK-SENSE-STATUS-CTRLWORD`), grades the feature controls after it (`CHK-SENSE-STATUS-CTRLWORD-FEAT`), and logs word 255 (`OBS-SENSE-STATUS-CTRLWORD`) as an open question.

|Does a withdrawal whose TREADY-low cycle is the first cycle of a pending DATA read, before the sampler enters the request state, end that read through the stream-error state? Does a withdrawal in the cycle in which the timeout expires also set TIMEOUT_ERR, and does it add one or two to COUNT_BAD? The text returns SLVERR on a stream error only if the active FSM is requesting or assembling, and gives the stream error priority over the timeout. So no check can say which stored words belong to a slot. (hw/ip/key_manager/doc/architecture.adoc, section "KPV Scrambling"; hw/ip/scrambler/doc/architecture.adoc, section "Address Scrambling"; hw/ip/key_manager/doc/firmware.adoc, section "Boot Sequence".) `sep_km_key_vault_alloc_rand_test` (CHK-REVOKE) and `sep_km_handover_rand_test` (CHK-HO-KPV-SHRED) log the count of stored words that change and grade the CTRL effects only.
